## ✅ 2026-07-20 (same session, continued): open-ended overnight pattern discovery — result is "underpowered," not "clean negative" — caught and corrected before reporting

User corrected the framing of the immediately prior task: not "does the RTH pattern transfer overnight" but genuine open-ended discovery — what precedes a big overnight move, how it behaves once underway, and whether any of it predicts the following RTH session, with no pre-conceived shape. Dispatched to Gemini across 3 parts (precursors, move-evolution, RTH-linkage — 7 concrete candidate hypotheses total), with mandatory 70/30 chronological train/test split, permutation testing, N≥20-in-test floor, and day-clustering checks specified up front.

**Gemini's report: 0 of 7 candidates survived.** Audited the actual results file (not just the summary) before accepting this — found the persisted `RESEARCH_CLAIM` mislabeled `rigor_status: 'clean'`, which the underlying data flatly contradicts: **every single one of the 7 candidates failed the N≥20-per-bucket floor** (test-bucket sizes as low as n1=1/n2=23, n1=0/n2=0) **and every single one showed `day clustering clean: false`.** Root cause: only ~82 "large overnight move" days exist in the full available history (80th-percentile threshold, ~410 total sessions), and the 70/30 holdout leaves ~25 test-period events — a further binary feature split fragments that into single-digit-to-teens per bucket, structurally unable to clear N≥20 regardless of whether a real pattern exists.

**Corrected the persisted claim** to reflect this honestly: this is a **structurally underpowered test design**, not a clean absence-of-signal finding. Some candidates do show a genuine train-to-test sign flip (e.g. "Overnight UP → RTH continues UP": train +0.158 vs test -0.167) which is real evidence against those specific effects independent of the power problem — but the blanket "0 of 7" headline should not be read as "overnight patterns don't exist."

**Real, useful methodological finding**: the ONE overnight precursor pattern that previously DID clear rigor (`globex_large_moves_start_near_pit_safe_levels`, levels-proximity, N=80) used a full-sample 5,000-draw permutation test against ALL qualifying days, not a chronological train/test holdout — that approach has more statistical power for a rare-event population this size (~82 events total) and is the better template for any further overnight-pattern mining, not the holdout design used this pass. **If this thread continues**: re-test the same 7 candidates (or new ones) with a full-sample permutation design instead of a 70/30 split before concluding anything is genuinely absent — not done this session, flagged for whenever the user wants to keep pulling this thread.

## ✅ 2026-07-20 (same session, continued): does the user's confirmed RTH taper-timing pattern have an overnight analog? Tested honestly, real negative

User's RTH domain knowledge ("down moves happen fast then fizzle around 11am-noon, up moves grind all session") was already confirmed for RTH (`backtest_directional_taper_timing.mjs`, 2026-07-19). Asked whether the same asymmetry shows up overnight, explicitly flagging they don't trade this session and would rely on the system to characterize it. Scoped and dispatched to Gemini with the out-of-sample discipline built into the spec from the start (given the overnight-calibration overfitting lesson from earlier the same session), not bolted on after.

**Built** (`scripts/backtest_overnight_taper_timing.mjs`): reused the existing 80th-percentile large-overnight-move threshold (364pt, N=411 days, same method as `backtest_globex_move_levels.mjs`) and a rolling-extremum walk to find each qualifying move's own taper point (last new directional extreme before RTH open), bucketed into 5 natural overnight landmarks (pre-Globex, evening, Asia, London, pre-market). Landmark selection derived strictly from the first 70% of days (chronological); the frozen result evaluated only against the held-out last 30%, never used to pick anything.

**Audited before trusting** (read the actual script, not just the summary — same standing discipline, doubly warranted after the overfitting retraction earlier today): threshold/detection reuse confirmed correct, `computeRigor`/`recordClaim` imported and used properly (not hand-rolled), landmark-selection logic requires a real ≥70% DOWN-taper rate before even being considered a candidate, and the final verdict requires a >10-point train-vs-test margin plus N≥20 both sides before calling anything positive — a real, non-trivial bar, not a rubber stamp.

**Result: negative.** Training-set best candidate (evening block, 18:00-midnight ET) showed a promising-looking 16.7% DOWN vs 0.0% UP split in-sample — but on the untouched test set (DOWN N=28, UP N=24, both clearing N≥20): 7.1% vs 4.2%, a 2.9-point gap, nowhere near the 10-point bar for a real finding. Day-clustering clean and stable on both sides. **The RTH asymmetry (fast-down-then-fizzle vs grind-up-all-session) does not transfer to the overnight session** — a real, useful negative, not an inconclusive one. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `globex_directional_taper_asymmetry` recorded.

**Not done / natural next steps if this thread continues**: this tested one specific framing (taper timing against fixed clock landmarks). Doesn't rule out other overnight characterizations — e.g. whether overnight moves that are STILL extending at RTH open predict anything about the following RTH session (a different question than "when does it stop"), or a finer landmark grid than the 5 broad blocks used here. Flagged for a future session if the user wants to keep pulling this thread; not chased further today given the honest negative result and the amount of ground already covered this session.

## ✅ 2026-07-20 (same session, continued): overnight recalibration idea tested — real descriptive fact, but the P&L "edge" was pure overfitting, caught and retracted before being reported

User's hypothesis: overnight NQ moves slower, so the flat 90/40 stop/target (borrowed from RTH, used throughout today's wider-window work) needs recalibrating for overnight-fired touches, and some specific levels probably do better overnight. Dispatched to Gemini with a pre-built script (`scripts/backtest_overnight_calibration_20260720.mjs`) computing pooled MAE/MFE percentile distributions split RTH-fired vs overnight-fired, deriving an overnight-specific stop (p75 MAE)/target (p50 MFE), and re-simulating every overnight-fired touch under both the flat default and the derived calibration.

**Gemini's headline result** (re-ran the same deterministic script myself first — numbers matched exactly, so no transcription error): RTH excursions p75 MAE/p50 MFE = 149.8pt/91.0pt vs Overnight = 127.8pt/65.3pt — genuinely narrower, confirming the "overnight is slower" part of the hypothesis. But the P&L comparison (flat 90/40 = $5,226 vs "calibrated" 127.8/65.3 = $36,858.50, pooled past year, N=3,730) is where the real problem was: **the stop/target was derived from the exact same touches it was then tested against — in-sample fitting**, the same overfitting pattern already documented for `backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs`'s original broken version.

**Built the out-of-sample check before trusting or reporting anything** (`scripts/backtest_overnight_calibration_oos_20260720.mjs`): trained the same derivation on the first 70% of the year only (177 days), then tested the resulting fixed stop/target (117.8pt/56.8pt) against the held-out last 30% (76 days, N=1,078) never seen during calibration. **Result: flat 90/40 = $7,502 vs trained-calibration = $7,625 — a $123 delta, statistically indistinguishable from noise.** The ~$31,632 in-sample "improvement" was almost entirely overfitting, not a real, generalizable edge. The specific per-level "genuine overnight edge" claims Gemini persisted (`PD_IB_MID`, `CAM_S2`, `PD_LOW`, `PD_HIGH`) were never individually OOS-tested either and don't survive the pooled OOS result.

**Corrected the record before this ever reached the user as fact**: `OVERNIGHT_NARROWER_EXCURSION` kept as `CONFIRMED` but narrowed to a pure descriptive fact (the excursion distributions really are different) with an explicit note that no P&L edge is implied. `ON_LEVEL_EDGES_CALIBRATED` marked `STALE`/retracted with a full account (not silently deleted), per this codebase's standing retraction convention. **Bottom line for the user's actual question**: yes, overnight genuinely moves differently — but simply widening the blanket stop/target to match doesn't produce a real, tradeable edge once tested honestly. If there's a genuine overnight edge for specific levels, it isn't this one; would need proper per-level OOS testing (not the pooled blanket-parameter approach used here) to find it, and that hasn't been done yet.

**General lesson**: a dramatic in-sample number (7x P&L improvement here) is exactly the kind of "surprising result" the standing "vet before trusting" rule exists for — the fix (a simple 70/30 chronological train/test split reusing the same deterministic script) took under 10 seconds to run once built. Worth building the OOS split into `backtest_overnight_calibration_20260720.mjs` itself as a standing check if this line of work continues, rather than treating it as a one-off audit step.

Delta: **-$589/-$1,895/-$1,444** — more trades every time (333-567 more), but net worse P&L every time.

## ✅ 2026-07-20 (same session, continued): "24hr trading cycle" past-year prop walkthrough — modest positive, but only after finding and fixing 3 rounds of contamination in the process

Direct follow-up to the real-N prop walkthrough above. User asked to see the wider-window ("24hr trading cycle") idea's performance on a prop account over the past year specifically. Before building anything, re-verified the underlying wider-window backtest more thoroughly than the earlier same-day pass — this materially changed the conclusion.

**Re-checked the 59-level prior-period table, not just the 6 same-day-forming rows checked earlier today**: spot-checked `CAM_R1`, `FLOOR_PIVOT`, `PW_HIGH` (both directions) independently. First attempt disagreed badly with the original report (wrong window direction — extended forward past close instead of backward into the overnight session; confirmed the correct direction via the doc's own `ONH`/`ONL` caveat language). Second attempt, after fixing the direction, still disagreed on win rate by 15-25 points across the board — root-caused to a flat 240-bar (4hr) resolution cap starving overnight-fired trades of the time they need to resolve (confirmed directly: `RTH` showed 11/238 `EXPIRED` outcomes, `WIDE` showed 122/352 — overnight NQ moves slower, and a clock-time cap calibrated for RTH pace was cutting resolution short and silently converting would-be wins/losses into `EXPIRED`/pnl=0). Third attempt, resolving to the window's own natural end instead of a flat cap: **5 of 6 spot-checked rows now reproduce the original report closely** (`CAM_R1_SHORT` remains a real, unexplained outlier — sign-flipped, N roughly halved — flagged, not chased further). **Re-ran the same fix against the earlier same-day-forming-levels check (`IB_HIGH_SHORT`/`IB_LOW_LONG`)**: N was unchanged (279/249, confirming the earlier disagreement there wasn't a maxBars artifact) but EV flipped sign again between attempts (+$2.63→-$3.75) — if anything this *strengthens* the original "confirmed unreliable" conclusion for the same-day-forming table, since the result is visibly unstable to small, reasonable methodology choices.

**Built the actual past-year walkthrough** (`scripts/backtest_24hr_prop_walkthrough_1yr_20260720.mjs`) using the now-validated corrected methodology, scoped to prior-period levels only (52 level names — excludes the confirmed-unreliable same-day-forming 6, plus `ONH`/`ONL`/`3M_VAH`/`VAL`/`POC` per their existing documented caveats), simulating touches over the full overnight+RTH window and wrapping the results in the same chronological DLL/lockout prop-account mechanic used elsewhere in this codebase.

**Found and fixed 3 more rounds of real contamination while building it, each one meaningfully inflating the reported P&L**:
1. `IB_MID`/`OR_MID` — separate, genuinely same-day-forming `level_prices` rows distinct from the already-excluded `IB_MID_SCALP`/`OR_MID_AFTER_IB` — missed in the first exclusion list. (`IB_MID_LONG` was the single top performer, $3,461 of the reported total, before being caught.)
2. `RTH_VWAP`/`WEEKLY_VWAP`/`MONTHLY_VWAP` — live-accumulating period-to-date values, not fixed before the scan window starts. `WEEKLY_VWAP` was already documented elsewhere in CLAUDE.md as same-day-forming (for an unrelated reason — minute-bar-scanner conditioning) but the original wider-window doc had still treated it as "prior-period safe"; excluded here on the more conservative, correct standard.
3. `DAILY_OPEN`/`WEEKLY_OPEN`/`MONTHLY_OPEN` — not prior-period at all: a day's/week's/month's own open isn't known until that period's own RTH session begins, so testing a touch of "today's open" during the *preceding* overnight is a lookahead violation, structurally identical to the `IB_MID`/`OR_MID` problem just caught. These were the #1/#2 contributors (~$5,100 combined) in the pass immediately before this fix.

**Final result** (after all 3 corrections): $200 DLL → $5,862 (N=1456, WR 69.8%, PF 1.08, expectancy $4.03/trade), $400 → $4,006 (N=1836, WR 69.1%, PF 1.04, expectancy $2.18/trade), $600 → $2,412 (N=1972, WR 68.7%, PF 1.02, expectancy $1.22/trade). **Net positive at every DLL level, but modest and thin — not a clear win**, especially compared to the P&L swings seen during the correction process ($25k+ → $9-11k → $2-6k across the 3 fixes). This is a SIMULATION, not real trade history (only the 4 dedicated Globex setup_types have ever fired this way live, and those were already checked today at N=4-6, too thin to compare). Given 3 rounds of self-caught contamination during a single build, this result is reported with explicit residual-uncertainty caveats — a 4th undiscovered issue is plausible, and this should be treated as a directional estimate, not a final number. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `24hr_trading_cycle_1yr_prop_walkthrough_prior_period_only` recorded (30-day recheck).

**General lesson reinforced twice in one session**: a candidate scan/eligibility list built by pattern (grep a naming convention, copy an existing "safe" list) needs a fresh, direct check against the live data's actual distinct values before trusting it — `level_prices` has several near-duplicate-looking names (`IB_MID` vs `IB_MID_SCALP`, `OR_MID` vs `OR_MID_AFTER_IB`) where only one of each pair is actually safe, and a name matching a "looks prior-period" pattern (`DAILY_OPEN`) can still be same-day-forming in substance. `SELECT DISTINCT level_name FROM level_prices` before assuming any hand-built inclusion/exclusion list is complete.

## ✅ 2026-07-20 (same session, continued): prop-account walkthrough answers "is it ready" — honest answer is no, the fully-validated roster is too thin and net-negative

User asked directly whether Gemini could do a "prop walkthrough," given today's real-N suppression fix (see the entry below) had just narrowed the live roster to 5 setup_types. Scoped it as a 2-scenario comparison on top of the existing chronological 2-year walk-forward infrastructure (`scratch/backtest_prop_2yr_walkforward_CORRECTED_TARGETS.mjs` as the base): **LEGACY_ROLLING** (the old `n>=20 && ev>=-5` eligibility every prior walk-forward used, no `real_n` check) vs **CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER** (only setup_types whose latest live `SETUP_STATUS` recommendation is NOT `SUPPRESS`/`THIN_N` — a static current-snapshot filter applied retroactively, explicitly caveated as such, same convention as `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA`/`NL30`). Swept DLL at $200/$400/$600 (6 runs total). Dispatched to Gemini.

**Gemini's process hit its 20-minute timeout, but the actual script and results file had already been written to disk ~18 minutes earlier and were complete** — per the standing "if `agy` times out but left a real script, run/use that instead of re-dispatching" convention, used the existing output rather than re-running from scratch.

**Audited before trusting**: found the new script dropped the 18-setup target-correction resimulation step the base script had (a scope gap in my own dispatch instructions, not a Gemini error) — this explains why `LEGACY_ROLLING/$400`'s $19,416.15 doesn't match the previously-published $29,095.64 (corrected-targets) or $21,152.01 (original, pre-correction) numbers; it's a legitimate different baseline (raw `actual_pnl`, no correction), not a bug. Independently cross-checked the load-bearing `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER` numbers directly via SQL: `IB_BULLISH`'s day-type-gated 16-trade subset and `IB_BEARISH`'s 22-trade subset are each a small, plausible fraction of their full unfiltered 2yr populations (121 and 123 trades respectively) — the gating mechanism is doing something real, not producing a nonsense subset.

**Result**: `LEGACY_ROLLING` looks healthy at every DLL ($200→$27,678.77, $400→$19,416.15, $600→$11,602.63 cumulative, 3,034-4,001 trades — tighter DLL wins, consistent with the already-known "$75-ish DLL is a local optimum" direction). `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER` is **flat -$954.50 at every DLL level** (N=38, WR=52.6%, PF=0.77, expectancy -$25.12/trade) — DLL never binds hard enough to matter because there are only 38 trades in the entire 2-year window. Only `IB_BULLISH` (N=16, -$423.50) and `IB_BEARISH` (N=22, -$531.00) contributed anything; `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_BALANCE_FADE`/`_TREND` and `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` fired zero qualifying trades in the whole window. Real-N tracking itself is only 11 calendar days old (`origin_status` history starts 2026-07-08).

**Honest conclusion, stated plainly**: the system is **not prop-account-ready today** on a strictly real-N-validated basis — not because the underlying setups are all bad, but because almost the entire historically-healthy-looking roster was calibrated on `BACKFILL` (synthetic) data that the 2026-07-20 real-N fix correctly stopped trusting, and what's left (2 day-type-conditional setups with a combined 38 real-window trades) is thin and net-negative. This isn't a new problem introduced today — it's the same "~80% of `active_setups` is synthetic" fact this codebase has documented since 2026-07-17, now made concrete in dollar terms for the first time via a real walkthrough. **Not a dead end**: `real_n` keeps accumulating for every `THIN_N` setup_type going forward (they still fire `SHADOW`, still resolve, still count toward `real_n`) via the existing generic `backtest_setup_status.mjs` weekly recalibration — no new mechanism needed, just time and continued live/SHADOW operation. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `current_validated_roster_2yr_walkforward_net_negative` recorded (30-day recheck — re-run once more setup_types have accumulated real_n≥20). One real, unresolved oddity surfaced along the way, not chased further this session: `IB_BEARISH`'s day-type-gated subset is a net loser (-$531/22) even though its full unfiltered population is net positive (+$1,926.50/123) — the rolling day-type-bucket gate is selecting a worse-than-average subset for this setup specifically, worth a closer look in a future session.

## ✅ 2026-07-20 (new session, post-`/clear`): worked all 3 pending OPEN_DECISIONs + 2 overdue RESEARCH_CLAIMs — 1 real bug fixed in the recheck mechanism itself, 1 live fix shipped, 1 backtest finding downgraded from "unverified" to "confirmed unreliable"

Picked up exactly where the prior session's `/clear` left off — 3 `PENDING` `OPEN_DECISION`s and 2 `RESEARCH_CLAIM`s flagged overdue by the session-start hook, all from the same 2026-07-19/20 target-calibration/window-mismatch work. All 5 resolved or meaningfully advanced this session; nothing left silently pending.

**1. Fixed a real bug in the RESEARCH_CLAIM mechanism itself, caught while rechecking.** `scripts/test_extension_vs_garch_vol.mjs` and `scripts/test_extension_vs_next_level_distance.mjs` (2026-07-19 vintage) hand-rolled their own `performance_audit` INSERT instead of calling the canonical `recordClaim()` helper (`scripts/record_claim.mjs`) — and set `next_recheck_due` to the SAME DAY the claim was written, not +30 days, which is why the session-start hook flagged both as "overdue" the very next day. Fixed both to call `recordClaim()` (matching this codebase's own standing convention); re-ran both, same negative findings hold (GARCH r=0.0507, next-level-gap r=0.0450 — consistent with the original 0.0476), now with a real 30-day recheck date.

**2. Globex PD-level stop/target miscalibration risk (`globex_pd_level_stops_calibrated_on_rth_only_data`, MEDIUM) — RESOLVED, confirmed theoretical not practical.** Dispatched to Gemini per user's explicit "use gemini" direction. Real Globex-fired N for all 4 affected setup_types (`PD_POC_FADE_LONG/SHORT`, `PD_VAH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_VAL_FADE_LONG`) is 4-6 each — far below N≥20, all real (`ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` origin, not `BACKFILL`). Audited before trusting: an apparent discrepancy against a fresh direct-SQL cross-check turned out to be MY OWN query bug (didn't filter `performance_audit.window_days`, which has 5 rows per signal_name per run — 10/30/90/180/9999-day windows; Gemini had correctly used the 9999/all-time row throughout). `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `globex_pd_level_stop_calibration_risk_theoretical` recorded. Recheck when any of the 4 types' Globex-fired N approaches ~15-20.

**3. Wider-window level-fade backtest (`wider_window_level_fade_backtest_findings_20260720`, HIGH) — NOT resolved, narrowed and made more serious.** User wanted the two flagged-as-risky rows (`IB_HIGH_SHORT`, `IB_LOW_LONG`) checked before any live RTH-gate change. Built `scripts/verify_ib_wider_window_20260720.mjs` — imports the real `resolve()` from `backtest_unified.js`, uses canonical `level_prices` values, RTH-only side verified byte-exact against the committed script's own `--dry-run` (N=6/N=19 match). **The wider-window side does NOT match Gemini's report**: Gemini had `IB_HIGH_SHORT` N=93/EV=+$22.67, `IB_LOW_LONG` N=98/EV=+$13.79; independent reimplementation gives N=279/EV=+$2.63 and N=249/EV=**-$0.28** — a 3x N discrepancy and a sign flip on one row. Root cause is structural, not a simple bug: `detectLevelFades()` hardcodes an RTH-only bar filter internally and cannot be called with a wider window at all, so testing any of the 6 same-day-forming levels' wider window requires a bespoke reimplementation outside the real function — exactly the "two independent reimplementations disagreeing" failure mode already documented for `classifyRegime()`. **Practical conclusion: treat the entire 12-row same-day-forming-levels table as unreliable, not just the 2 originally-flagged rows** — excluded from consideration for any live change. The separate 59-level prior-period-levels table is a different, more mechanically sound case (no same-day-formation dependency) and was NOT the subject of this re-check — still only spot-checked on its RTH-only side. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `ib_wider_window_reimpl_disagreement` recorded. `OPEN_DECISION` re-flagged (still PENDING, HIGH) with this narrowed scope — remaining real question is whether to build the RTH-gate widening for the 7 clear-winner prior-period levels only (`DAILY_OPEN_LONG/SHORT`, `IB_HIGH_LONG` [not `_SHORT`], `3M_VAL_SHORT`, `3M_POC_SHORT`, `WS1_SHORT`, `PM_POC_SHORT`, `PM_HIGH_LONG`), none of which are implicated by today's finding. Full account: `docs/WIDER_WINDOW_BACKTEST_20260720.md`.

**4. INVALIDATED setups never getting `actual_pnl` (`invalidated_session_closed_setups_never_get_actual_pnl`, MEDIUM) — RESOLVED, shipped live.** User confirmed: yes, mark-to-market POST_ENTRY invalidations (setup was live ≥2min before a structural break — a real trader could've been in the trade), same convention as the TIME_EXPIRED fix from the prior session. Fixed `structurallyInvalidateSetups()` (`server/routes/acd.js`) to compute `actual_pnl` via `isLongSetup()`/`entry_zone`/current price/`LIVE_INSTRUMENT` constants whenever `invalidationTiming==='POST_ENTRY'`, setting `resolution_method='MARK_TO_MARKET'`/`actual_outcome='INVALIDATED'`. `PRE_ENTRY` invalidations stay null on purpose (no real entry ever happened). Backfilled the 11 pre-existing historical POST_ENTRY rows with null `actual_pnl` (`scripts/repair_invalidated_post_entry_pnl_20260720.mjs`, backup `active_setups_invalidated_pnl_backup_20260720`) using the nearest known price at/before `resolved_at` as the mark-to-market exit — all 11 recovered. `SESSION_CLOSED` rows deliberately left untouched (a separate, still-open question, not part of what was approved this session — the decision's own text already distinguished these).

Verified end-to-end: `test_invariants.mjs` clean except one pre-existing failure (`IB_BEARISH` EV drift, confirmed via `git stash` to predate this session, unrelated to any change made here), `npm run lint` clean, server healthy (`/api/health` 200, no new `server_errors.jsonl` entries). ## ✅ 2026-07-20: Made target calibration visible + fixed the Unified Signal Table's disconnected stop/target display

Follow-on from the 2026-07-19 target-calibration work, prompted by the user asking "how would I know the targets are learning and being checked" and "are these easy to spot in the unified table."

**Visibility built**: `GET /api/acd/target-calibration-coverage` + `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx`'s new "Target Calibration Coverage" section — shows the full guardrail-exclusion funnel (why 92/110 setups don't use corrected-resim: plateau/thin-tail/OOS/rigor, each with real examples) and a "widening evidence" panel comparing every corrected setup's old (order-blind, truncated-MFE) target against its new one. All 18 corrected-resim setups widened, none narrowed, +10% to +297%. No hardcoded thresholds — every number is a setup compared against its own prior calibration.

**Real bug found and fixed in the Unified Signal Table** (`/api/performance-audit/unified`): stop/t1/t2 were never actually connected to `OPTIMAL_STOP` — they came from a 14-entry hand-typed `overrides` literal (the "never hand-type a stat" anti-pattern, 8th documented instance) or a raw MAE/MFE percentile fallback. 13 of 14 override entries were already fully dead (real `OPTIMAL_STOP` data existed and would have taken priority once wired correctly). Removed the map entirely. A second, deeper bug surfaced while wiring the real data in: the endpoint's shared query joined on `MAX(run_date) GROUP BY signal_type` (not `signal_name`) — any `OPTIMAL_STOP` signal_name that lagged the group's most recent run (7 of them) was **silently absent from the result set entirely**, not just stale. Fixed with a dedicated `DISTINCT ON (signal_name)` query, the same pattern CLAUDE.md's own OPTIMAL_STOP hard rule already prescribes. `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` (real stop=53/target=35) was the confirmed live casualty — silently missing before, correct after. Added a `targetMethod` field + a "✓ CAL" badge on the `t1` column so corrected-resim rows are now visually distinguishable. `TRT_LONG` (the one setup with no real calibration, N=16) now honestly shows `--` instead of the removed hardcoded guess.

**Audited the rest of the endpoint for the same anti-pattern** (user: "check thoroughly"): found one more hardcoded map, `levelMap`. Its `bestCtx` field is a correctly-designed fallback (`describeLevel()` only uses it when real `win_rate`/`sample_size`/`ev_per_trade` are absent) — not a violation. Its `freq` field ("~1/day" etc.) IS hardcoded and shown unconditionally regardless of real data — lower severity than stop/target/WR/EV since it doesn't drive any trading decision, flagged but not rebuilt this session (would need a real "touches per trading day observed" computation not currently available anywhere).

**Real finding while investigating why `TRT_LONG` was hardcoded** (user: "TRT_LONG used to be a great setup, not sure why it was hardcoded"): it genuinely has strong real performance (75% WR, +$56.63/trade) on the 16 trades `SETUP_STATUS` counts — but 27 of its 44 real historical `active_setups` rows (dated 2026-02-17 through 2026-05-28, so recent/ongoing, not stale historical debris) are `status='RESOLVED', resolution='EXPIRED', actual_pnl=NULL, resolved_at=NULL` — real trade attempts whose outcome was never actually captured. Ruled out `backfill_mae_mfe.mjs` (never touches `resolution`/`status`/`actual_pnl`) and a TRT-specific custom resolution block (none exists, unlike `ABSORPTION_LONG`/`COIL_SURGE`) as the cause. Root cause not yet found — flagged as `OPEN_DECISION` `trt_long_expired_rows_missing_actual_pnl` (MEDIUM) rather than chased further in the same session as the explicit hardcoded-audit request. If recoverable via a real bar-walk replay, `TRT_LONG` could plausibly cross the N≥20 floor and reveal a genuinely strong, currently-invisible live setup.

Verified end-to-end: `test_invariants.mjs` clean, lint/build clean, both new/fixed panels confirmed live via Playwright with real data, no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.

## ✅ 2026-07-19: Built and wired the breakeven-then-trail exit mechanism live (SHADOW-only) — `promote_scaleout_runner_cam_r4_fade_short` OPEN_DECISION resolved

Picked up the HIGH-priority pending decision per its own §10 build sequence (docs/SCALEOUT_RUNNER_SPEC.md). Built the full pipeline: 4 new `active_setups` columns (`runner_trail_width`, `breakeven_armed_at`, `runner_peak_price`, `runner_trail_price` — additive migration, no backup needed), a single-phase extension to `resolveSetupsByPrice()` (`server/routes/acd.js`) that walks bars exactly like `scripts/backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs` (arm on target touch, snap to breakeven, ratchet-only trail, same-bar-arm-and-breach edge case, 4PM ET session-close force-resolve), and an unconditional `resolveSetupType()` diversion so every `FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT` touch now becomes `FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT_TRAIL` (keeps the base type's own calibration frozen/clean, per spec §4).

**Real gap found while wiring the INSERT path**: the standard `_suppressedSetups`-based ACTIVE/SHADOW gate (`server/routes/acd.js`, the level-fade candidates path) only knows about types with a REAL `SETUP_STATUS` row — a genuinely brand-new type (zero live history) is *absent* from that set, which reads as "not suppressed" and would have fired straight to `ACTIVE` on its very first touch, with N=0 live-resolved trades. Fixed by explicitly forcing `SHADOW` for any `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS` entry with a `trailSignalName` field (i.e., any trail-mechanism variant), regardless of the standard suppression check — matches CLAUDE.md's "New setup type checklist" item 3. `runner_trail_width` is read live from `performance_audit` (`signal_type='BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST'`, signal_name `A_FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT`) at insert time, never hardcoded.

**Verified before trusting** (per spec §10 step 3): wrote a standalone script that copy-pasted the exact new acd.js walk logic and ran it against real historical `FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT` trades + real `price_bars_primary` data, using the same stop=50/target=61.3/trail=15.5 the backtest used. Aggregate EV matched the backtest's own stored `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` value to 4 decimal places ($23.4456, N=147, scratches=2) — strong confirmation the mechanism is a faithful reproduction, not a close-enough approximation. One minor discrepancy: `t1Reached` count was 75 vs. the backtest's stored 77 — traced to the backtest's own session-end check comparing a *UTC* hour against 16 (its code comment expresses uncertainty about this — "let's use 20:00 UTC" — but the actual comparison used 16, not 20/21) while the new live code correctly compares the ET wall-clock string. Net dollar impact of the 2-trade classification difference is exactly zero (aggregate EV still matches to the penny), so not a live-code bug, but worth fixing in the backtest script itself in a future pass so the two stay in lockstep rather than agreeing on EV by coincidence of cancellation.

**`scripts/test_invariants.mjs` check `[1]` updated**: it previously assumed every `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS` entry follows the fixed-stop/fixed-target `SETUP_STATUS`+`OPTIMAL_STOP` convention. Trail-mechanism variants don't (dynamic exit, calibrated via `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` instead, and legitimately have zero `SETUP_STATUS` history pre-launch since they're force-SHADOWed regardless) — check now branches on `meta.trailSignalName` rather than failing a structurally-correct bootstrap state. Clean run after the fix (0 failures, 2 pre-existing/expected warnings about the base type's `SETUP_STATUS` still showing `ACTIVE` — expected, since the base type's own row just goes stale/frozen rather than being actively suppressed, given the diversion is unconditional not conditional like the `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` precedent).

`node scripts/backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs` added to `run_weekly_backtests.sh` (also fixed its output path — it was writing to `scratch/antigravity_response.md`, Gemini's live dispatch file, which a weekly cron would have clobbered; moved to `scratch/breakeven_trail_report.md`). `lint`/`lint:frontend`/`build` all clean. Server restarted, `/api/acd/setup-detection` returns 200 with no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`. `server/schema.sql` regenerated (143 tables/views). `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `active_setups` row and schema-source note updated. Display wiring: `SETUP_DISPLAY_LABELS['FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT_TRAIL']` and `SETUP_RESOLUTION_TEXT.TRAIL_EXIT` added — a fuller "armed, trailing Npt" live card UI (spec §7) is deliberately **not** built this pass, since the variant is forced SHADOW-only right now and produces no live banner/alert regardless (SHADOW rows don't fire cards); worth doing once promotion is under consideration.

**Not done, deliberately deferred**: the other 5 backtested survivors (`DAILY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`, `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S2_FADE_LONG`, `PD_POC_FADE_LONG`, `PW_HIGH_FADE_LONG`) — spec §10 step 6 explicitly says build one at a time, starting with the largest-N survivor, which is what happened. Promotion to `ACTIVE` for `FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT_TRAIL` itself is also deliberately not automatic — needs N≥20 live-resolved trades and a manual decision, same as every other new setup type.

`OPEN_DECISION` `promote_scaleout_runner_cam_r4_fade_short` resolved via `node scripts/flag_decision.mjs --resolve promote_scaleout_runner_cam_r4_fade_short '...'` (see decision text for the full resolution note). Closes the loop per the standing no-dead-ends checklist: persisted (`active_setups` + `performance_audit`), has a recheck path (`run_weekly_backtests.sh`, generic `backtest_setup_status.mjs`/`update_optimal_stops.mjs` will auto-discover once live data accumulates), wired (SHADOW, not just flagged), discoverable (`ARCHITECTURE.md` + `CLAUDE.md` + this entry).

## ✅ 2026-07-19: Folded Monthly VWAP + Prior-Year value area into the system — both correctly SHADOW (THIN_N)

**What triggered this**: user asked which VWAP/POC magnet variants were tested. Found two genuine gaps — monthly VWAP and yearly POC had never been computed or tested at all (not "tried and dead" like PM_POC/2D_POC, just never built). User: "Yea fold them ib to our system," followed by "I want to make sure its done correctly and in a way that will be used a[nd] learn and grow" — held to the standing no-dead-ends checklist explicitly for this reason.

**Built**: `MONTHLY_VWAP` (month-to-date, live-accumulating like `WEEKLY_VWAP`) and `PY_VAH`/`PY_VAL`/`PY_POC` (prior COMPLETE calendar year VA — safe prior-period convention like PW/PM, deliberately not 3M's date-inclusive pattern) in `scripts/compute_levels.js`, with a `yearBounds()` helper and a memoization cache (the year-long VA is identical for every date within the same current year — avoids ~650x redundant ~98K-row queries). Backfilled `level_prices` for all 417 historical dates, verified complete directly (not just the console summary) — the only nulls (21 dates in Nov–Dec 2023) are legitimately correct, since their "prior year" (2022) has zero data in this database.

Built `scripts/backfill_monthly_vwap_yearly_poc.mjs` (historical touch-detection for `active_setups`, modeled on `repair_weekly_vwap_window_mismatch.mjs`) to bootstrap calibration data, since neither level had ever fired historically. **Two real bugs found and fixed while building this, both worth remembering**: (1) declared `runMae`/`runMfe` but never updated them in the initial draft — caught before ever running it; (2) used an initial 100pt placeholder stop/target box, which turned out to genuinely truncate both `mae_points` and `mfe_points` for these longer-period magnets (37.5% of trades hit the ceiling, p75/p90 clustered right at ~100-117pt, confirmed by directly querying the resulting distribution) — the same truncated-MFE bug class already documented elsewhere in this file, caught live in the act this time rather than after the fact. Widened to 300pt (verified this reduced ceiling-hits to ~5-12% and pushed p75 well clear of the boundary) and re-ran. Also found the update pipeline (`update_optimal_stops.mjs`) requires `replay_resolution`, not `resolution` — my backfill only set the latter, so zero `OPTIMAL_STOP` rows got written on the first pass; fixed by setting `replay_resolution = resolution` (the backfill's own bar-walk already IS a genuine resimulation, exactly what that column is supposed to mean).

**Real finding** (not a bug, an honest result): with truncation fixed, most touches of a month/year-scale magnet do **not** resolve (stop or target) within a single RTH session — `MONTHLY_VWAP` has a real touch sample (96 LONG/80 SHORT) but only 12-17 same-day-*decided* outcomes, below the N≥20 floor. `PY_VAL` has 0 touches and `PY_POC` has 1 over ~2.3 years — a full year's value area is wide, and price rarely revisits the exact center of a specific bygone year. All 4 correctly classified `THIN_N` by `backtest_setup_status.mjs` (auto-discovered, zero hardcoding needed).

**Wired into `acd.js`'s live candidates array** (`keepLevelsAll`) — verified safe to do so first: checked the insert-site logic directly (`acd.js` ~line 6061) and confirmed `THIN_N` types ARE included in `_suppressedSetups` (same set as `SUPPRESS`), so these correctly fire `SHADOW`-only, never a live alert, and will accumulate real forward data via the existing standard promotion path. Verified a documented, sensible fallback stop/target (90pt/40pt, `acd.js` line ~4423) exists for exactly this "no calibration yet" case before wiring, so a live SHADOW touch won't use garbage. Confirmed live: hit `/api/acd/setup-detection` directly post-deploy, 200 OK, no crash. `node scripts/test_invariants.mjs` clean (only the same pre-existing, unrelated `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` warning, traced via git log to 2026-07-17). Updated `ARCHITECTURE.md` (level count 64→68, coverage paragraph) and `CLAUDE.md`'s "Where to look." No `SETUP_DISPLAY_LABELS` entry needed — confirmed every consumption site already falls back to a humanized `type.replace(/_/g,' ')` when no explicit label exists, same as `WEEKLY_VWAP_FADE`/`PW_POC_FADE` already do.

**Closes the loop cleanly per the standing no-dead-ends checklist**: persisted (`level_prices`, `active_setups`, `SETUP_STATUS`), has a recheck path (the already-scheduled, generic `backtest_setup_status.mjs`/`update_optimal_stops.mjs` — no new cron needed, they auto-discover), wired (SHADOW, not just flagged-and-ignored), discoverable (`ARCHITECTURE.md` + `CLAUDE.md` + this entry). Nothing further required unless/until real live SHADOW data accumulates toward N≥20 — check back via the standard `SETUP_STATUS` promotion path, not a special-cased follow-up.

**Two new standing hard rules added to CLAUDE.md today, worth knowing before touching any backtest script**: (1) a comparison's baseline must be computed by the exact same method as what it's compared against, never read from a stored/precomputed field — this is how the `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` finding got retracted; (2) a backtest candidate grid needs a floor derived from the underlying data's own resolution (e.g. bar range), not just statistical guardrails — this is how the scale-out thread's spurious 32-survivor batch got caught.

## ✅ 2026-07-19: Scale-out thread finished thoroughly — found and fixed a new overfitting class (bar-noise trails), landed on 1 strongly cross-validated finding

User pointed out the $7,943.63/$2,298 walk-forward numbers had gotten conflated with two OTHER, mostly-unrelated threads: "curbing losses" (wider stops — tested, net loser for 5/7 setups, never promoted) and "letting winners ride" (scale-out — real but only validated for 1 setup, not wired). Asked to finish the scale-out thread properly.

Widening the scale-out grid for thoroughness (fractions 0.1-0.9, trail 25th-95th percentile) surfaced 32/96 "survivors" — but 31 picked a 3-6.8pt trail, and the median 1-min NQ bar range is 6.25pt: those trails sit within normal single-bar noise, which every existing statistical guardrail (thin-tail/plateau/OOS/rigor) passed cleanly since none of them check data resolution. Added a `CLAUDE.md` hard rule for this new failure class, fixed with a data-derived floor.

Dispatched to Gemini to properly separate a "snug" tier from a "real runner" tier and cross-validate against `LEVEL_CONTINUATION`/`POST_RES_SEQ` (the connected threads the user asked to fold in). Audited before trusting: **Tier A (snug) washed out completely — 0/96 survive**, confirming the floor fix. **Tier B: 2 survivors.** `CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT` (N=39, rigor-clean) is now independently corroborated by two separate, previously-persisted signals (`LEVEL_CONTINUATION`: 61.9% of its failed fades continue 50pt+; `POST_RES_SEQ`: 43.1% favorable-first) — verified these numbers directly against the DB, not just Gemini's prose. `PW_HIGH_FADE_LONG` also clears every guardrail but N=16 is thin, reported as suggestive not confirmed. The earlier marginal survivor `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG` correctly dropped out under the stricter methodology. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`/`OPEN_DECISION` updated to this final result; superseded `SCALEOUT_RUNNER_TEST` rows cleaned up. Still not wired live. Full account: [docs/TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md](TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md).
