## ✅ 2026-07-19: Checked the live suppression pipeline for the same bugs — clean, but found a real staleness consequence instead

Closes `OPEN_DECISION` `live_setup_status_pipeline_likely_has_same_ev_bugs`. Read `backtest_setup_status.mjs` directly rather than leaving it flagged. **It's clean**: EV is computed straight from `AVG(actual_pnl)` (already commission-exact, no dpp approximation) with no `mae_points`/`mfe_points` candidate sweep at all — neither of today's two bugs apply.

But that check surfaced a real, different consequence: `actual_pnl` for historical trades reflects whatever target was live *when each trade fired* — so a setup whose target just got corrected has its SUPPRESS/PROMOTE decision computed against stale, pre-correction history. Confirmed concretely: **`PD_LOW_FADE_LONG` is currently SUPPRESSED** (EV=-$15.01/trade under its old 60pt target) despite its corrected 143pt target showing one of the *largest* walk-forward improvements of all 19 (+$3,480, trade count 11→74). 5 more are ACTIVE but borderline (within $10 of the -$5 SUPPRESS bar): `PD_POC_FADE_LONG`, `FLOOR_R2_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG`.

**Verified this self-heals, no further bug to fix**: `liveStats._opt` (which now holds the corrected target) is read for both ACTIVE and SHADOW candidate construction in `acd.js` — so `PD_LOW_FADE_LONG`'s SHADOW trades going forward will use the corrected target, and the existing PROMOTE mechanism (N≥15 in the 90-day window, WR≥52%, EV>0 — a deliberate anti-fabrication floor) will recover it automatically once enough data proves the improvement. Not instant, but not a dead end either. Added a session-start hook section (`CORRECTED-TARGET-BUT-SUPPRESSED WATCH`) making this visible every session rather than leaving it to be rediscovered by accident.

## ✅ 2026-07-19: The corrected target methodology is wired LIVE — 19/103 setup_types now use it, durably

Closes the target-calibration thread's last remaining item: `OPEN_DECISION` `promote_18_layer1_corrected_targets` is RESOLVED. Full account in [docs/TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md](TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md). Short version:

Extracted the corrected methodology (chronological resimulation, thin-tail/plateau/OOS/rigor guardrails) into a new shared service, `server/services/targetCalibrationService.js` — refactored `backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs` to use it (verified byte-identical results before trusting the refactor), then wired it into `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs` itself: every setup_type now gets the corrected target attempted after its stop is computed via the existing EV-sweep, using it when it clears every guardrail (tagged `notes.method='corrected-resim'`) and falling back to the unchanged existing methodology otherwise. This is what makes it durable instead of a one-time patch — every future scheduled run (weekly + daily) re-evaluates every setup_type automatically, so nothing silently reverts and newly-qualifying setups get promoted without a manually-maintained list.

**A real discrepancy surfaced when running it live**: only 17 (not 18) setups survived on the first live run. Root-caused precisely: `CAM_S2_FADE_LONG` flipped from surviving to failing purely from the live pipeline's pre-existing exclusion of `TIME_EXPIRED` trades — itself a second instance of the exact truncation bug this whole thread targets (a `TIME_EXPIRED` trade can be correctly re-evaluated by the corrected methodology's own 390-bar walk). Fixed by giving the corrected-target call its own wider, `TIME_EXPIRED`-inclusive trade population. Re-ran: **19/103 setup_types** now use the corrected path.

Updated `test_invariants.mjs` check `[5]` to re-derive `corrected-resim` rows via the real `computeCorrectedTarget()` function before trusting them (not a hand-copied comparison — matches the standing discipline this check was built with). Full self-check clean: all 103 `OPTIMAL_STOP` rows match a fresh re-derivation (0 mismatches), lint/build clean, no new errors, pre-existing hardcoded-pattern flags verified via `git blame` to predate this session, git status clean.

**Remaining open**: `OPEN_DECISION` `live_setup_status_pipeline_likely_has_same_ev_bugs` (HIGH) — the separate live suppression pipeline (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`) has not been checked for the same class of bugs and is the biggest unexamined risk from this whole thread.

## ✅ 2026-07-19: Old-vs-new 2yr walk-forward comparison run — Layer 1 fix quantified end-to-end, then found commissions weren't handled exactly

Closes the target-calibration thread's original ask ("once these are fixed and answered can we compare old vs new walkforward"). Before running it, a second stale-data bug from the same family as `SCALEOUT_RUNNER_TEST` was caught: `target_sweep_v2`'s first broken run had persisted all 103 tested setups unconditionally; the corrected rewrite only touched the 18 survivors, leaving 85 setups' original spike-picked rows (including the literal `PD_LOW_FADE_SHORT`=719.8pt garbage) sitting in the table with the same `run_date`. Caught via a sanity-check on the walk-forward's own setup count, deleted the 85 stale rows.

Ran the comparison, then the user asked two sharp follow-ups that each caught something real:
- **"3800 trades is a lot, I figured letting trades run would mean fewer."** Verified directly (not just re-asserted): 335 of the ~400 additional trades (84%) concentrate in exactly the 18 corrected setups, and every one of them shows more trades — several dramatically (`PD_LOW_FADE_LONG` 11→74). Mechanism confirmed: the day-loop's rolling-EV eligibility filter (`n≥20 && ev≥-$5`) was suppressing these setups because their OLD, understated target kept their historical rolling EV below the bar; correcting the target un-suppresses them on far more days. Remaining ~16% is a DLL-lockout-timing cascade. Also surfaced a bigger implication worth its own thread: since this eligibility filter runs on the same EV methodology that had two real bugs, the **live** `SETUP_STATUS` suppression pipeline likely has the same issue right now, for setups beyond just these 18.
- **"Are you including commissions in PnL?"** No — every script in this thread (`backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs`, `backtest_scaleout_runner.mjs`, the walk-forward comparison) used a flat empirically-derived $/pt multiplier instead of the exact live formula (`points*$2 - $1 commission`, verified against `server/config/instruments.js`, whose MNQ commission is itself independently verified against a real broker statement per its own header comment). Fixed all three to use the exact formula. Re-ran everything: same 18 `target_sweep_v2` survivors (magnitudes shifted down slightly), scale-out survivors dropped from 4 to 3 (`CAM_R2_FADE_LONG` no longer clears the bar — its baseline was only $1.43/trade, thin enough that a uniform -$1 correction tipped it under), walk-forward improvement corrected from +44%/$9,372 down to **+37.6%/$7,944** (still real, still strong). Caught a third stale-row instance mid-fix (same pattern, `CAM_R2_FADE_LONG`'s numbers were byte-identical to the pre-fix run) — this time fixed the root cause instead of cleaning up again: both scripts now self-clean (`DELETE ... WHERE NOT (signal_name = ANY(survivors))`) after every run.

Final numbers: **cumulative P&L $21,152.01 → $29,095.64 (+$7,943.63, +37.6%)**, trade count 3,462→3,863, win rate 61.7%→53.9% (expected), max drawdown +$494. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`s and `OPEN_DECISION`s updated to final figures. Nothing wired live. Full account: [docs/TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md](TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md).

## ✅ 2026-07-19: Fixed target_sweep_v2's overfitting, then found and fixed a much bigger baseline bug in the scale-out script — VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT finding retracted, CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT is the real one

Full narrative in [docs/TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md](TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md). Short version: fixed `backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs`'s spike-picking (thin-tail gate + a candidate grid anchored to the current live target, chronological OOS split, plateau check, same methodology already validated in the scale-out script) — 18/103 setups now survive, a believable rate.

While cross-checking that fix against the earlier scale-out finding, found `backtest_scaleout_runner.mjs` had been comparing against the WRONG baseline: it read `OPTIMAL_STOP.ev_per_trade` (the live stored value) instead of resimulating it. That stored value is computed by the old order-blind sweep against `mae_points`/`mfe_points` captured under whatever stop was live when each trade historically fired — for `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT`, historical stops ranged 15-28pt+ vs. today's 10pt, so the stored -$1.99 baseline was comparing today's tight stop against MAE data bounded at a much wider historical boundary. A properly self-consistent baseline is actually **+$15.22/trade** — not a loser. **This reverses the finding**: the scale-out config ($17.76 full/$14.81 OOS) actually loses out-of-sample against the true baseline ($15.22/$18.65).

Fixed the script to always resimulate its own baseline, tightened the promotion gate to require beating baseline both full-sample AND OOS, re-ran clean: **4/96 setups survive** (`CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT` — real, strong, N=39, $6.32→$14.27 full / $0.42→$10.10 OOS; `PW_HIGH_FADE_LONG` — thin, N=16; `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG` and `CAM_R2_FADE_LONG` — marginal). `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` is no longer a survivor at all. Retracted its `OPEN_DECISION`/`RESEARCH_CLAIM` (with a full account of what was wrong, not silently deleted), recorded new ones for `CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT`. Independently spot-checked `target_sweep_v2`'s own baseline (matched a from-scratch check within rounding) — confirmed it does NOT have the same bug, its 18 survivors are trustworthy on this front.

**General lesson**: auditing a backtest's simulation logic isn't the same as auditing where its baseline comes from — a comparison is only as trustworthy as its least-audited side. Added to CLAUDE.md's hard rules.

## 🆕 2026-07-19: Scale-out + trailing-runner design — a real, narrow, out-of-sample-validated finding for one setup; full dialogue now tracked in a dedicated spec doc

Continuation of the target-calibration thread below. Full reasoning trail (not just conclusions) now lives in [docs/TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md](TARGET_CALIBRATION_SPEC.md) per explicit user request to keep documenting the dialogue, not just findings — this entry is the short version.

Attempted a corrected version of the live target sweep (`scripts/backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs`) using genuine chronological resimulation and candidates drawn from the real, untruncated MFE distribution. **It failed its own sanity check**: several setups picked absurd "best" targets off a single outlier trade (`PD_LOW_FADE_SHORT` picked 719.8pt with `targetHits=1` of N=38). Conclusion: a single static wider target is structurally the wrong tool — it can't be both reliably-hit and tail-capturing.

Pivoted to a **partial scale-out + trailing-runner** design instead (take partial profit at the real T1, let the remainder ride with a trailing stop rather than a second fixed target — sidesteps needing to predict in advance whether a trade is "a big one," which both tested Layer-2 predictive triggers already failed to do). This independently converges with a 39+-day-old parked brief (`project_scaleout_optimization_parked.md`). Dispatched the combinatorial exploration to Gemini with the sweep's own overfitting failure written in as a required guardrail.

**Result, audited** (`scripts/backtest_scaleout_runner.mjs`, one real bug found and fixed — `new Date().toISOString()` instead of `CURRENT_DATE` for `run_date`, the same forbidden pattern flagged elsewhere in this codebase): of 96 setup_types tested with strict overfitting guardrails (thin-tail T1-reach floor, plateau check, chronological out-of-sample split, rigor check), **only 3 (3.1%) survived** — a believable, non-suspicious pass rate, unlike the earlier 88/90 target-sweep result that turned out to be a data artifact. Of those 3: **`VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` is genuinely strong** — baseline was actually a LOSER (-$1.99/trade at 100%-at-T1), a 50%-at-T1/50%-runner-with-16pt-trail config blends to +$17.76/trade, **+$14.81/trade out-of-sample**, N=28, rigor-clean, stable across chronological thirds. `OPEN_DRIVE_SHORT` is fragile (OOS collapses to $2.40 from in-sample $33.67) and `CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT` is marginal (+$0.61/trade over baseline) — neither should be acted on yet.

Flagged `OPEN_DECISION` `promote_scaleout_runner_value_area_responsive_short` (MEDIUM) — wiring this live requires real new engineering (a trailing-stop state machine, no existing analog in `acd.js`), not a parameter tweak. Nothing wired live. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `scaleout_runner_value_area_responsive_short` recorded.

**Follow-up, same session**: user asked how they'd track the health of all these new systems once wired live. Confirmed `node scripts/audit_pipeline_freshness.mjs` already answers this generically (queries every distinct `signal_type`, no hardcoded list — verified it auto-picked up every signal_type created this session with zero code changes) but wasn't part of the standing self-check sequence, so it was easy to forget to run — added as step 8 to the "Self check" hard rule. For the scale-out mechanism specifically, if/when it's wired live: flagged in the `OPEN_DECISION` above that it needs its own live health instrumentation (is the trailing-stop state machine actually firing/resolving correctly, not just "did a batch script run this week") built in the same commit as the mechanism itself, not as a follow-up.

## 🆕 2026-07-19: "How should live target recalibration work" — confirmed stops are already distribution-calibrated, targets are not; first two live-trigger candidates both tested negative

Follow-up to the OPTIMAL_TARGET blind-spot finding above. User asked directly: are targets calibrated off the std dev of large extensions, and what would a live "monitor for large moves and recalibrate" trigger look like.

**Confirmed precisely how `update_optimal_stops.mjs` actually works** (read the real code, not assumed): stops ARE already percentile/distribution-calibrated (`sweepOptimalStopAndTarget` sweeps candidate stops off that setup_type's own p25/p40/p50/p60/p75 `mae_points` percentiles — genuinely follows the no-static-threshold convention). **Targets are NOT** — `sweepOptimalTarget` picks from a fixed point grid (`TARGET_SWEEP = [10,15,20,25,30,35,40,50,60,70,80,90,100,120,150]`), capped at `p75_mfe`, which is exactly the truncated MFE distribution flagged in the OPEN_DECISION above. User's instinct (calibrate off σ of real large extensions) is the right fix and directly IS the OPEN_DECISION's principled path — not a new idea, confirmation that it's the correct one.

**Framed as two separate layers** (batch recalibration vs. live per-trade trigger — genuinely different problems, don't conflate them):
- **Layer 1 (batch, no live monitoring)**: extend `mfe_points` with the real post-resolution walk (already built), compute the σ/percentile distribution of the corrected extension, feed that into a re-run of the real `sweepOptimalTarget` instead of a hand-picked multiplier. This is the OPEN_DECISION's fix — unchanged recommendation, now with a name ("Layer 1") for future reference.
- **Layer 2 (a live signal that says THIS specific in-progress trade looks like a big one)**: needs a real, rolling-distribution-derived trigger, not a hardcoded rule. Tested the two cheapest, already-available candidates:
  - **`GARCH_VOL_SCALE`** (already live/persisted) vs. post-target extension size: **NEGATIVE**, Pearson r=0.0507 (N=4,710). Bucket means barely move (LOW-vol 135.8pt / NORMAL 135.8pt / HIGH-vol 147.7pt). Ruled out. (`scripts/test_extension_vs_garch_vol.mjs`, `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `garch_vol_predicts_target_extension_size`)
  - **Distance to the next known `level_prices` level beyond target** ("room to run") vs. extension size: **NEGATIVE**, r=0.0476 (N=6,092). Quartile means show a small monotonic trend (tightest-room Q1=112.2pt → most-room Q4=140.0pt) but far too weak/noisy to drive a live decision alone. Notable side-finding: Q1's gap range is only 1-3pt — a quarter of all target-hit trades already have some tracked level within 1-3pt of target, reflecting how densely the 65-level universe packs (same density this session's Globex placebo test already had to control for). (`scripts/test_extension_vs_next_level_distance.mjs`, `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `next_level_gap_predicts_target_extension_size`)

**Both rigor-clean** (no day-clustering, stable across chronological thirds) — these are real, honest negatives, not thin/noisy non-results. **Not yet tested**: time-of-day (the taper-timing data already hints at a real morning/afternoon asymmetry, cheap to test next) and live momentum/velocity into the target (most genuinely "real-time" candidate, also the most novel and expensive to build/validate — deprioritized until the cheaper candidates were exhausted). **Current state: Layer 1 (the batch recalibration fix) remains the highest-confidence next step — no validated Layer 2 live trigger exists yet.**

## 🆕 2026-07-19: Expanded wider-target test to all 90 qualifying setups (not just the original 7) — found the near-universal result exposes a real structural blind spot in OPTIMAL_TARGET itself

User asked why only 7 setups got the wider-target EV test and whether more could use it — the honest answer was that 7 was an arbitrary hand-pick ("kept the 7 with the most robust samples" from the favorable-first ranking), and the setup list was hardcoded, which would have gone stale the next time `backtest_post_resolution_sequence.mjs` re-ran (the exact hardcoded-list anti-pattern this codebase already has a standing rule against). Fixed `backtest_wider_target_ev.mjs` to pull its setup roster dynamically from the live `POST_RES_SEQ` `TARGET_*` rows (N≥20, already gated at write time) instead of a fixed list, then re-ran it against all 90 qualifying setups.

**Result: 88 of 90 (97.8%) show positive EV improvement at a 1.5x wider target with the SAME original stop** (only `CAM_R3_FADE_SHORT` -$0.32 and `PD_IB_MID_FADE_LONG` -$6.13 are flat/negative). Only 15.4% of the 5,625 re-simulated `TARGET_HIT` trades flipped to a loss.

**That result is too broad to take at face value as "88 independent alpha discoveries" — and root-causing it found a real mechanism, not noise.** `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s `sweepOptimalTarget()` — the function that produces the LIVE `optimal_target` every setup actually uses — picks its target by scanning `mfe_points` from `active_setups`. That column is written by `resolveSetupsByPrice`'s bar-walk, which **terminates the instant the original stop or target is hit** (the same resolution-truncation property this session already found and built the whole post-resolution thread around). Direct spot-check on `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_SHORT`'s 6 most recent `TARGET_HIT` trades: target distance is a consistent ~48.5-50pt, and `mfe_points` is barely past it every time (50.25, 57.5, 56, 67, 57.5, 65.5pt) — the walk stops almost immediately after crossing target, so the sweep that chose that 48.5pt target literally never saw whether price kept running. **The near-universal "widening helps" result across unrelated setup_types is the same blind spot surfacing 88 times, not 88 separate discoveries.**

Flagged as `OPEN_DECISION` `optimal_target_blind_to_post_resolution_continuation` (HIGH priority) rather than just noted here, since it's a real fork: (a) the principled fix — extend `mfe_points` (or add a parallel column) using the same post-resolution bar-walk `backtest_post_resolution_sequence.mjs` already does, then re-run the REAL `sweepOptimalStopAndTarget()` against the corrected data so `OPTIMAL_TARGET` reflects the true achievable move, not a hand-picked 1.5x/2x multiplier bolted on after the fact — or (b) decide the arbitrary multiplier is good enough and promote it for the 88 setups as-is. **Nothing from this thread is wired into live `sizeMultiplier`/`OPTIMAL_STOP`** — `WIDER_TARGET_EV_TEST` rows remain backtest-only findings pending that decision.

**Long vs short asymmetry, tested directly** (user's domain claim: "down moves happen quickly and continue ~2hrs, then try to retrace 11am-noon... up moves grind all day, need conviction to hold through retracements"). Two independent checks:
- Trade-weighted across all 90/68 setups (not just eyeballing the top of a ranked list, which is dominated by SHORT-direction setup names and overstates the effect): TARGET_HIT favorable-first rate is 46.2% for SHORT-direction setups vs 42.7% for LONG (N=2,676 vs 2,949 trades) — directionally consistent with the claim but modest, not dramatic.
- Built `scripts/backtest_directional_taper_timing.mjs` to test the timing claim directly: for STOP_HIT trades, split by whether the adverse extension is a down-move-against-a-LONG or an up-move-against-a-SHORT. **LONG stop-outs (down moves) taper off faster**: median 106 min to stop getting worse, 94.8% finish by noon (N=1,262). **SHORT stop-outs (up moves) take longer**: median 158 min, only 90.2% finish by noon, and 9.2% (vs 2.9% for LONG) are still extending after 1pm (N=1,008). Real, rigor-clean (no day-clustering, stable across chronological thirds), and directionally exactly what the user described — but both directions still mostly resolve by noon, so it's a difference of degree, not the down-move-fast/up-move-all-day dichotomy in its starkest form.

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): Post-resolution research thread resolved into a real, validated "let winners run" finding — plus a new level-continuation finding and a Globex dispatch in flight

Continuation of the post-resolution excursion thread above. Three more pieces, in order:

**1. Corrected the time-of-day test a second time** (`scripts/backtest_post_resolution_sequence.mjs`) — added a `SUBSTANTIAL_EXTENSION_PTS=50` filter (matching the level-continuation script's own "real move, not noise" threshold) so trivial 2-3pt ticks don't count the same as genuine continuations. Result barely changed: **92.7% of morning stop-outs with a real continuation finish extending adverse by 12:00 ET** (N=2,270, down from the uncorrected 3,283). Still concentrated in the "before 10am" bucket (1,718/2,270) — most real continuations complete within the same early-morning window rather than stretching the full ~2 hours the user described, worth keeping in mind rather than treating this as a clean 1:1 confirmation.

**2. Built the wider-TARGET mirror of the wider-stop EV test** (`scripts/backtest_wider_target_ev.mjs`, `signal_type='WIDER_TARGET_EV_TEST'`) — scoped to the 7 `TARGET_HIT` setups with the highest favorable-first sequencing rates. Unlike the stop-side test (net loser for 5/7 setups), **this came back decisively positive for all 7**, properly accounting for the real risk (trades flipping from win to loss when price reverses before the wider target):

| Setup | Current EV | 1.5x Target EV | Flipped-to-loss (of N) |
|---|---|---|---|
| FLOOR_PIVOT_FADE_SHORT | $13.91 | $56.62 (+307%) | 4 |
| WPP_FADE_LONG | $14.11 | $39.99 (+183%) | 9 |
| PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_SHORT | $22.61 | $49.33 (+118%) | 8 |
| PD_OR_MID_FADE_SHORT | $18.96 | $39.41 (+108%) | 12 |
| CAM_R1_FADE_SHORT | $11.57 | $23.88 (+106%) | 19 |
| CAM_R2_FADE_SHORT | $15.54 | $28.20 (+81%, worse at 2x) | 11 |
| WEEKLY_VWAP_FADE_SHORT | $1.19 | $19.05 (huge relative) | 25 |

**This is the real, validated version of the "let winners run" hypothesis the user's original MFE observation was pointing at** — not the earlier misleading 300-475% "extra run-up" numbers (which conflated generic 4-hour volatility with a real, capturable pattern), but a proper re-simulation showing genuine EV improvement even after accounting for trades that would flip to losses. **Not yet wired into live `sizeMultiplier`/`OPTIMAL_STOP`** — a real, promising finding sitting ready for a deliberate promotion decision, same standing caveat as everything else from today.

**3. New finding: certain levels disproportionately "kick off" large continuations when their fade fails** (`scripts/backtest_level_continuation_magnitude.mjs`, `signal_type='LEVEL_CONTINUATION'`) — ranked 68 setup_types (N≥20) by how often a failed fade leads to a real (50pt+) continuation. Top: `OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_LONG` (85.3% of failures continue 50pt+, avg 187.8pt extra), `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` (81.5%, 182.5pt), `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_SHORT` (81.5%, 123.7pt), plus the Camarilla S1/S2/S3/R1/R2 family and `DAILY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`. All rigor-clean. A real candidate for an "early warning" signal independent of whether you're trying to fade the level at all.

**4. Globex large-move-vs-level-proximity — dispatched to Gemini, audited, hardened, resolved positive** (`scripts/backtest_globex_move_levels.mjs`, `signal_type='GLOBEX_LEVEL_PROX'`, `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `globex_large_moves_start_near_pit_safe_levels`): does the same "certain levels kick off big moves" pattern hold during the Globex/overnight session — the part of the day this codebase's RTH-only setup roster structurally cannot touch, and which a prior session already found drives 90% of huge (500+pt) rotation days. Full spec (data-derived threshold, inflection-point detection, PIT-safe level scoping matching the `3M_*`/`WEEKLY_VWAP` lookahead-risk exclusions, placebo/null comparison) written into `scratch/claude_request.md` and dispatched.

Gemini's script (audited line-by-line before trusting): threshold derivation (80th pct of Globex range = 364.00pt, N=410 days), inflection-point detection (rolling-extremum walk), and PIT-safe level scoping (verified exact match against the dispatch's 49-always-safe + 2-conditional-of-65 INCLUDE/EXCLUDE list) were all correct. **But its placebo comparison drew exactly ONE random timestamp per day (unseeded `Math.random()`)** and reported 63.7% vs the real 76.3% as "significantly more often than random chance" — a two-proportion z-test on that single draw actually gives z=1.73, p=.085, **not** significant at the conventional bar. Same pattern as the Regime C debunking earlier today, but this time auditing the *methodology* (not just re-checking the conclusion) mattered more than auditing the number itself.

**Fixed by replacing the single draw with a proper 5,000-draw permutation test** building an empirical null distribution (rewrote the canonical script; re-run and reproduced independently). Result: **the finding is real, and Gemini's single draw had actually understated it** — null mean 65.6% (sd 4.81%), real 76.3% sits at z=2.22 above the null, empirical p=0.018. N=80 large Globex moves (>=364pt), rigor-clean (no day-clustering, stable across chronological thirds: 0.77/0.85/0.68). Top contributing levels: `PD_CLOSE` (17 of 61 hits), `CAM_R2`/`CAM_R1` (11, 9), `FLOOR_PIVOT` (9), `PD_POC`/`PD_SESSION_MID` (7 each) — prior-day close and Camarilla/floor-pivot reversal zones dominate, not weekly/monthly levels. Effect is real but modest (~11pt gap on a dense baseline of ~49 active levels/day) — **not a standalone trade signal at this N**, and this codebase's setup roster is still RTH-only so nothing fires on it live; it's a research finding about *where* big Globex moves tend to originate, not yet an actionable setup. Persisted `PROVISIONAL` (real, N=80 is workable but not huge — recheck in 30 days per the `RESEARCH_CLAIM` lifecycle, and consider whether this is worth building into an actual Globex-session setup type given the roster is currently RTH-only).

## 🆕 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): Post-resolution excursion research — real methodology, one bug ruled out, one real flaw found, not yet rebuilt

Started from a sharp user observation: `active_setups.mae_points`/`mfe_points` correctly survives a retracement WITHIN an open trade (it's a running `Math.max()`), but the bar-walk (`acd.js` `resolveSetupsByPrice`, ~line 299-332) terminates the instant stop or target is hit — so anything that happens AFTER resolution (a stopped-out trade recovering, a winner continuing to run) is structurally invisible to the stored data.

**Built and ran** (`scripts/backtest_post_stop_recovery.mjs`, persisted `signal_type='POST_STOP_RECOVERY'`): re-walked 3,537 `STOP_HIT` trades 240 bars (~4hr) past their stop. **34.3% overall would have reached the original target anyway.** Real per-setup variation, 61.7% (`CAM_S2_FADE_LONG`) down to 3.8% (`BRACKET_BREAKOUT_LONG`) — `C_STANDALONE_DOWN` at 7.3% usefully cross-validates as a genuinely-wrong-not-just-tight-stopped setup, consistent with its already-known SUPPRESS status.

**Correctly did NOT stop there** — recovery rate alone doesn't prove a wider stop is a net win, since a "recovering" trade might dig a deeper hole first (getting stopped at the wider level too, just for more). Built `scripts/backtest_wider_stop_ev.mjs` (persisted `signal_type='WIDER_STOP_EV_TEST'`) to properly re-simulate the 7 highest-recovery setups with 1.5x/2x wider stops, full EV accounting (unaffected trades untouched, recovered trades become wins, still-losing trades become BIGGER losses). **Result: widening the stop is a net loser for 5 of 7 setups** (`PD_IB_MID_FADE_LONG`: -$14 to -$20/trade despite 61.1% recovery rate), only marginally positive within noise for 2. The existing EV-optimized stops were already close to right — the recovery-rate metric alone would have been actively misleading if acted on directly.

**Then tried the winner-side mirror** (`scripts/backtest_post_target_runup.mjs`, persisted `signal_type='POST_TARGET_RUNUP'`) — and this is where a real flaw was caught before being reported as a finding, not after. Raw numbers looked way too good (+300-475% "extra run-up" beyond target across 90 setup_types, every single one "rigor-clean"). Checked the obvious culprit first (an entry-price convention mismatch between the live and backfill MAE/MFE paths, the same bug class documented elsewhere in this file) — ruled out directly, `entry_zone_high - entry_zone_low` is exactly 0 for every row, no discrepancy there.

**The real flaw**: the script only recorded the two EXTREME points (max favorable, max adverse) over the 4-hour window, with no record of which happened FIRST. `post_target_max_adverse` was consistently comparable to or larger than the "extra run-up" itself (e.g. `PD_IB_MID_FADE_LONG`: +116pt extra favorable vs. 133.6pt max adverse) — meaning the numbers can't distinguish "price ran further in our favor, then gave some back" (real, capturable opportunity) from "price dropped hard against us first, and only recovered to a new favorable extreme much later" (you'd have been shaken out or badly drawn-down before ever seeing the upside). Both scenarios produce similar summary statistics with this method. **Not fixed yet** — flagging the finding, not the conclusion; do not cite the +300-475% figures as a "let winners run" case, they don't currently support that claim either way.

### User's domain knowledge, given directly and worth testing rather than assuming true

- **Down moves**: happen quickly, continue for roughly 2 hours, and typically *try* to retrace starting around 11:00 AM-12:00 PM ET — "usually, but sometimes continues." A real, specific, testable time-of-day claim, not yet checked against data.
- **Up moves**: different character — can grind upward all day with minor retracements along the way; "you need conviction to hold through some retracements" rather than expecting a clean, fast, one-shot move like the downside tends to be.
- **The reframe that matters most**: instead of an absolute "extra points left on the table" metric (noisy, easily confused by generic volatility as shown above), the right question is an EFFICIENCY ratio — if the real move eventually reaches some true extreme, what fraction of that distance does the current target/stop actually capture? User's own bar: "we want to capture profit within 10% of the actual bottom if we're calibrated properly." A well-calibrated setup should show target_distance / eventual_true_extreme ≳ 90%, not some inflated "we could 4x'd it" framing.

### Next build (not started)

1. Track the actual SEQUENCE of new favorable/adverse extremes bar-by-bar after resolution, not just the two endpoint maxima — need to know order and timing (bar index + ET clock time), not just magnitude.
2. Test the 11am-noon down-move-retracement claim directly against the sequence data, as a hypothesis, not an assumption.
3. Compute the efficiency ratio (captured/eventual-true-extreme) as the primary metric instead of raw "extra points."
4. Likely needs separate treatment for up-moves vs down-moves given the described difference in character (fast-continue-then-retrace vs. grind-with-noise) — a single fixed window size for both directions was already shown to be the wrong assumption.

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): 2-year walk-forward built, then a real bug flipped the roster's P&L sign entirely

User asked for a 2-year extension of the existing prop walk-forward (`scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward_FULLYEAR.mjs`), dispatched to Gemini with full stat/MAE-MFE/per-setup/trade-export requirements. It came back showing a dramatic swing from the prior session's -$2,725.07 headline to +$22,768.95 cumulative — surprising enough (per the standing "vet a surprising result before trusting it" rule) to dig into before reporting.

Found a real, load-bearing bug: the script's day-loop was driven directly by `acd_daily_log` (`for (const dRow of tradingDays)` where `tradingDays` = that table's own rows), not by `active_setups`' own trade dates. `acd_daily_log` has real gaps (410 total rows across the ~2.7-year `active_setups` history) — confirmed 13 of 360 real trading days in the trailing 730-day window have no `acd_daily_log` row at all. Any missing date silently dropped **the entire day's trades** (every setup_type, not just the two `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` ones — `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` — that actually need `day_type`) from every regime's P&L. Those 13 days weren't random — they fell disproportionately in the most recent ~365 days and were evidently unusually active.

**Fixed in both scripts** (`backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward_FULLYEAR.mjs` and the new 2-year one): day-loop now driven by `active_setups`' own `DISTINCT trade_date`, `day_type` LEFT JOINed in (missing `day_type` only affects `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` eligibility on those 13 days, not other setups). Re-ran both fresh: trailing-1-year Regime A flipped from -$2,725.07 (N=1,990) to **+$18,707.33 (N=2,700)** — a complete sign reversal, not a minor correction. 2-year cumulative: **+$23,418.31 (N=3,486)**; Year 1 (2024-08-22 to 2025-08-22) +$7,911.34 (N=1,040); Year 2 (2025-08-22 to 2026-07-17) +$15,506.97 (N=2,446).

**Conclusion, opposite of last session's**: the current live-eligible setup roster, correctly simulated, is genuinely profitable over both the trailing 1-year and 2-year windows. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `walkforward_acd_daily_log_join_bug_flipped_pnl_sign`. Full report: `scratch/backtest_prop_2yr_walkforward_RESULTS.md` (per-setup breakdown, MAE/MFE, profit factor, streaks, origin_status disclosure — 99%+ of trades are still `BACKFILL`/`UNKNOWN` origin, this remains a strategy simulation not a record of real account history). Full trade export: `scratch/wf_2yr_all_trades.csv` (3,486 rows).

**Not yet done**: user asked for a further variant — no DLL at all until cumulative profit crosses $3,000, then a DLL applies but is explicitly NOT a trailing-EOD-style mechanism (interpretation being confirmed with user before building — could mean a flat/static DLL activating post-threshold, or could describe a different trailing-drawdown-conversion rule; asked rather than guessed given it changes the simulation design).

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): Order-flow/CVD — first real signal built, tested honestly, weak result

Picked up the order-flow gap flagged in `docs/REGIME_DETECTION_SPEC.md` §3.7 (delta/CVD divergence from price, never computed anywhere in this codebase before now). Verified data first, applying today's own hard-won lesson before building anything: `price_bars_primary`'s `bid_volume`/`ask_volume` columns are 100% populated back to 2022-12-14 including full Globex/overnight hours, and confirmed the same known ES contamination window (2023-11-15 to 2023-12-15) — filtered `symbol='NQ'` from the start this time rather than discovering the bug a third time.

Built `scripts/backfill_cvd_daily_history.mjs` — persists overnight CVD (prior RTH close to today's RTH open) and RTH CVD per day to `performance_audit` (`signal_type='CVD_DAILY'`, 417 days), using the exact same `ask_volume - bid_volume` convention already live in `caseEngine.js`'s `confirmedDeltaDir()` and `touchQuality.js`'s `classifyTouch()` — not reinvented.

**Tested Stage-1-style** (same discipline as the regime-detection work): does overnight CVD extremity predict the same day's RTH session, independent of any setup? **Result: no directional power** — HIGH_POSITIVE/HIGH_NEGATIVE overnight CVD buckets show no meaningful difference in RTH open-to-close return vs. NEUTRAL, and both are chronologically unstable. **A weak but real, clean, stable range-elevation effect does exist**: extreme overnight CVD (either direction) correlates with ~5-6% wider RTH range (327pt neutral vs 343-347pt extreme, N=70/56/232, all rigor-clean) — real, but much smaller than the already-live volatility regime classifier's range spread (216-508pt, a 2.3x range vs this signal's 1.06x). Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `overnight_cvd_weak_range_signal_no_directional_power`.

**Not yet tested**: the other order-flow angle from §3.7 — intraday CVD-vs-price divergence (price makes a new high, CVD doesn't confirm) as an exhaustion/reversal tell. Structurally different and bigger-lift (needs real touch/divergence detection, not just a daily aggregate test) — `CVD_DAILY` now exists as a real, persisted, reusable asset either way, so this isn't a dead end regardless of whether the divergence angle gets picked up next.

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): All-setups GARCH-scaled-stop test — 4 compounding bugs found and fixed, real but modest final result

Attempted to generalize the GARCH-scaled-stop idea from confluence zones to every individual level-fade `setup_type`, and to test whether it reduces EV variance across volatility regimes (user's hypothesis: "they should work good in different regimes"). Dispatched to Gemini; the attempt errored (`Error: timeout waiting for response`) but left a real finding worth taking seriously: when the frozen-baseline bug from the earlier consolidation was "fixed" to a self-consistent same-day denominator (`scale = pred_vol / that day's own unconditional variance`), the scale collapsed to **exactly 1.0000 for every single day** — meaning the fitted GARCH `alpha` (ARCH term) was reading as 0 across the board.

**Verified directly rather than trusted, and it led to real bugs, not a dead end:**
1. Confirmed the alpha≈0/scale≡1.0 finding was real (queried the persisted `GARCH_VOL_SCALE` table directly: 317/317 rows within 0.0004% of 1.0).
2. Found the actual cause wasn't "alpha is genuinely always 0" — neither the original confluence script nor Gemini's new persistence script filtered `symbol='NQ'` on the daily-bar query. `price_bars_primary` has documented ES contamination (2023-11-16 to 2023-12-14, from the standing data-sanity audit) sitting exactly inside the GARCH walk-forward warmup window — and because the fit uses an expanding window, that corrupted month stayed inside every subsequent day's training history for the entire ~3-year series. **This means the earlier "consolidated" confluence-zone GARCH result reported a few hours earlier the same day was itself also affected by this bug** (in addition to the already-identified frozen-baseline issue) — already correctly marked `STALE` in the research ledger, no further correction needed there.
3. Fixed the symbol filter, re-ran: alpha was now genuinely nonzero (mean 0.025, real variation 0→0.046) — but the scale swung wildly (0.0012 to 284.5x) on a subset of days. Root cause: when `alpha+beta` is very close to 1 (near-unit-root/IGARCH behavior), the unconditional-variance formula `omega/(1-alpha-beta)` divides by something near zero and blows up — a known numerical pitfall, not evidence the concept is worthless. Checked the actual distribution of `1-alpha-beta` across all 317 days before picking a threshold: a clean bimodal split (44 days essentially at 0, the other 273 sitting at a real 0.07-0.13) — not an arbitrary cutoff, a real gap in the data. Added a fallback (carry forward the last valid day's unconditional variance on a degenerate day) and re-ran: scale range became a sane 0.84-1.23.
4. Ran the actual all-setups backtest (`scratch/backtest_garch_all_setups.mjs`, Gemini-authored, audited before trusting) — found a 4th bug in the process: its `OPTIMAL_STOP` query had no `ORDER BY run_date`, so an unordered `SELECT` against a table with 800 historical rows (one per weekly run × 108 setup_types) could land on an arbitrary past calibration instead of the latest. Verified directly: 35/108 setup_types (32%) got a stale value, some by huge margins (`IB_BULLISH` stale=19pt vs. actual latest=79pt). Fixed with `DISTINCT ON (signal_name) ... ORDER BY signal_name, run_date DESC` — now a documented pattern in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Final, trustworthy result** (all 4 bugs fixed): of 103 setup_types with N≥20 valid trades, **28 show a rigor-clean all-time EV improvement** from GARCH-scaling (73 don't or get worse). Only 17 setup_types have enough regime-split data to test the "more consistent across regimes" hypothesis at all; of those, 8 show a reduced regime-to-regime EV spread, 9 don't — close to a coin flip, not the clean win the hypothesis predicted. **Only 2 of 103 setup_types show both** improved EV and reduced regime spread. Top improvements: `10D_IB_MID_FADE_LONG` +$9.68/tr, `PW_VAH_FADE_SHORT` +$7.07/tr, `DAILY_OPEN_FADE_LONG` +$5.83/tr. Real downside if applied blindly: `MONTHLY_OPEN_FADE_SHORT` -$15.40/tr, `FLOOR_S3_FADE_LONG` -$13.96/tr. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `garch_scaled_stop_all_setups_modest_minority_effect` — this supersedes the earlier (now-`STALE`) confluence-only claim as the first trustworthy version of this whole thread. **Not wired into live `sizeMultiplier`** — zero live-trading impact from any of this, the entire investigation happened at the backtest layer. If ever promoted, must be per-setup-type (the 28 that actually improve), never a blanket rule.

Promoted `scratch/backtest_garch_all_setups.mjs` → `scripts/backtest_garch_scaled_stop_all_setups.mjs` (fixed relative imports and the output path, added a header documenting all 4 bugs) per the same-day "no dead ends" hard rule — a script this useful, having survived this much correction, belongs in the permanent, discoverable location, not `scratch/`.

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): 7th instance of the hand-typed-live-stat anti-pattern found and fixed while consolidating the GARCH confluence-stop backtest

While checking whether `scripts/backtest_confluence_garch_stop.py`'s touch-detection (PROXIMITY=15, LOOK_FORWARD=30, FIXED_STOP=120) matched the live confluence-zone system before consolidating it, found `server/routes/keyLevels.js`'s `/api/confluence/today-zones` endpoint (feeds `App.jsx`'s live-rendered Confluence Zones card) was serving a **hand-typed `tierStats` object literal** despite its own comment claiming it was "latest weekly rigor-checked calibration." Compared against the real latest `performance_audit` `CONFLUENCE_AUDIT` row (`scripts/backtest_confluence.js`, run 2026-07-16): every tier was wrong by 3-6x, and `SINGLE` was wrong-signed (hardcoded +$6.91/tr EV vs. the real **-$3.02/tr**). This is the exact "Never hand-type a WR%/N/$ literal into a live-rendered card" anti-pattern already documented in `CLAUDE.md`'s hard rules with 6 prior instances — a 7th.

**Fixed**: replaced the hardcoded object with a live query against `performance_audit`'s latest `CONFLUENCE_AUDIT` row per tier, cached 12h (`server/lib/cache.js`, matching the "day-stable backend query" convention). Restarted `trading-journal-server.service` to pick up the change (it runs via plain `node server/index.js`, not nodemon — no auto-reload) and verified live: endpoint now correctly returns `SINGLE: -$3.02/51.2%`, `DOUBLE: +$0.25/51.3%`, `TRIPLE: +$3.61/50.0%`, `QUAD_PLUS: +$2.07/43.9%` instead of the stale `+$6.91/+$9.59/+$13.11/+$12.01`. Confirmed no frontend dependency on the dropped `clean` field before removing it; `npm run lint:frontend` clean.

## 🆕 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): GARCH confluence-stop backtest consolidated into one canonical script

Per the roadmap item in `docs/REGIME_DETECTION_SPEC.md` §6 (independent of the regime-detection thread — this is the separately-already-validated GARCH walk-forward vol-scaled stop from 2026-07-15/16 that was left sitting unconsolidated). Found only one `.py` file survives (`scripts/backtest_confluence_garch_stop.py`, with a `--last-150` CLI mode) — the "4 inconsistent scripts" language from the original mining session referred to 4 different RUNS with drifting ad-hoc parameters, not 4 separate files that needed merging.

Two real problems found and fixed in the consolidation: (1) the `--last-150` mode truncated the GARCH fit to only the most recent 150 days (100-day warmup) before evaluating — already diagnosed in this file's 2026-07-15/16 entry as noisier/less reliable than a full-history expanding-window fit, so retired rather than kept as an option; (2) the default mode's "recent" evaluation window was a hardcoded absolute date pair (`2026-06-12` to `2026-07-13`) that was already 5 days stale by the time this consolidation happened — replaced with dynamically-computed trailing 22-day and 50-day trading-day windows (matching this codebase's existing "last 22 sessions" convention, already used in `App.jsx`'s confluence card copy) so it can never go stale again. Touch-detection parameters (PROXIMITY=15, LOOK_FORWARD=30, FADE_TARGET=30, FIXED_STOP=120) re-verified against `scripts/backtest_confluence.js` and `keyLevels.js`'s live zone logic before consolidating — all consistent, no changes needed there.

**Re-run on fresh data, confirms the original finding cleanly.** All-time (both rigor-`clean:true`): TRIPLE N=9388 fixed EV=$11.96 → scaled EV=$14.48 (+21.1%); QUAD_PLUS N=8446 fixed EV=$15.04 → scaled EV=$17.86 (+18.7%) — same direction/magnitude as the original 2026-07-15/16 finding. Scaling improved EV in **every one of 6 tested tier/window combinations**, never hurt, including turning both tiers' currently-thin recent windows less negative or outright positive (last-50-day TRIPLE flips -$2.58→+$0.10; last-22-day TRIPLE improves -$14.79→-$11.75 without fully fixing what's currently a real losing stretch for that specific tier). Recent-window rigor flags real clustering (top5DayPct 55-90%) — expected for this signal class (level-fade touches aren't independent day-to-day, already documented elsewhere in this codebase), so treat the recent numbers as directional, not decisive; the all-time result (N>9000, both tiers, both clean) is the trustworthy one. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `garch_scaled_stop_confluence_zones_consolidated`. **Still not wired into live `sizeMultiplier`** — this is a validated backtest finding sitting ready for a deliberate promotion decision, not done here (that would need its own go/no-go, per the standing rule against silently wiring backtest findings into live code).

## 🆕 2026-07-18: Regime-detection research spec written + first real validation pass on Regime A/B/C — `regime_detection_methodology_needs_validation` narrowed, not resolved

User asked for a well-researched spec on regime-detection/anticipation methods for futures trading, explicitly inviting Gemini use. Wrote `docs/REGIME_DETECTION_SPEC.md`: inventories every regime-adjacent component already in this codebase with its real validation status (volatility regime = validated/live; day-type v1 = weak 41%/25% accuracy; day-type v2 = designed but never scored; Markov probability score = tested and rejected; per-level Hurst/ADF = validated for specific levels; GARCH walk-forward stop = validated but not consolidated), surveys the broader methodology space (structural-break/changepoint detection — CUSUM/BOCPD/`ruptures`; formal Markov-switching HMMs; GARCH-forecast-as-regime; intraday Hurst; ML ensemble classifiers, deliberately deprioritized given the ~350-400-day sample size; order-flow/CVD; calendar/expiry effects — none built, flagged as a real gap), and proposes a concrete 5-stage validation framework (independent predictive power → parameter sensitivity → out-of-sample/independent-ground-truth → multiple-comparisons correction → economic-rationale-last-not-first) as the actual mechanism that should have existed before Regime A/B/C got built last night.

**Then executed Stages 1-2 of that framework against Regime A/B/C directly** (dispatched to Gemini; its prose response hit the known response-file-truncation bug — see `feedback-gemini-cli-timeout-script-still-valid` memory, same failure mode, different trigger this time since it wasn't a CLI timeout — so the script it left on disk, `scratch/validate_regime_methodology.mjs`, confirmed to import the real `buildRegimeMap`/`computeRigor` rather than reimplementing, was re-run directly for clean output). **Real, differentiated result — not a uniform pass or fail**:
- **Regime C (persistence)**: cleanly validates. fresh→82.0%/established→76.0%/extended→73.0% 5-day same-label persistence, all N≥20 (319/37/48) and chronologically stable.
- **Regime B (price-stretch)**: mixed. `STRETCHED_LOW` validates (N=64, +$219.45pt fwd-5d avg, clean/stable, real independent mean-reversion bounce). `STRETCHED_HIGH` does NOT (N=28, wrong-signed in 2 of 3 chronological thirds, unstable).
- **Regime A (directional trend)**: does not clearly validate. `BULLISH_TREND`'s forward-5-day return (N=73, +$59.13pt) is smaller AND less stable than the unconditioned `NEUTRAL` bucket's (N=287, +$217.42pt) — undercuts the trend-continuation story. `BEARISH_TREND` has the right sign (N=44, -$60.37pt) but is unstable (driven by one bad third). Separately, the label *mechanism* is robust to reparameterization (25/90 and 40/150-day alt windows: 72.7%/63.4% day-agreement with live 30/120 config, zero outright BULLISH↔BEARISH flips in either) — so the classifier computes a stable label, it just hasn't been shown to predict much beyond baseline market drift.

Recorded as 3 `RESEARCH_CLAIM` rows (`regime_a_directional_trend_weak_independent_predictor`, `regime_b_stretched_low_validated_stretched_high_not`, `regime_c_persistence_validated_independent_predictor`, all `status='PROVISIONAL'`). **`OPEN_DECISION` `regime_detection_methodology_needs_validation` deliberately left PENDING, not resolved** — Stage 3 (true out-of-sample / independent-ground-truth validation) and Stage 4 (multiple-comparisons correction across the full regime × setup_type testing surface) are still unexecuted, and this pass's own forward-5-day windows overlap day-to-day (serial correlation, not corrected for) — a real caveat on every number above. Full detail, tables, and the caveat write-up: `docs/REGIME_DETECTION_SPEC.md` §7.

~~**Practical upshot for whenever regime-conditioned sizing is revisited**: Regime C and `STRETCHED_LOW` (half of Regime B) are now real, differentiated candidates worth Stage 3/4 investment; Regime A and `STRETCHED_HIGH` are not, as currently defined.~~ **Overturned same day, see below.**

## 🆕 2026-07-18 (same day, continued): Phase A follow-up — Regime C's "cleanest" result was a placebo artifact; no sub-label currently survives

User asked "is deeper research needed, how do we test this" — flagged a real methodological worry unprompted (does Regime C's persistence-decay pattern reflect real market structure, or is it a generic run-length statistical artifact?). Ran three tests directly (`scratch/validate_regime_phase2.mjs`, not dispatched to Gemini this time — two prior dispatches in this thread both needed a direct re-run anyway due to the response-file truncation bug, so there was no round-trip benefit left to capture for a task this precisely specified):

1. **Non-overlapping-window re-test** (fixes the serial-correlation caveat from the morning's pass): every Regime A/B trend/stretch bucket collapses below N≥20 once windows stop overlapping (`BULLISH_TREND` N=73→16, `BEARISH_TREND` N=44→9, `STRETCHED_LOW` N=64→12, `STRETCHED_HIGH` N=28→5). Only the two baseline/majority buckets (`NEUTRAL`, `NORMAL`) still clear the floor. **This codebase's ~410-day history does not currently contain enough independent trending/stretched days to validate or invalidate those specific buckets** — the morning's apparent confidence was inflated by non-independent overlapping samples, not real sample-size. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `regime_a_b_trend_stretch_buckets_underpowered_nonoverlapping`.
2. **Regime C placebo test** (200 permutations of `daily_score`, randomly shuffled to break all real temporal structure, same z-score/tercile construction rebuilt fresh on each shuffle): the real fresh/established/extended persistence percentages (71.8%/75.7%/72.9%) all land near the *center* of the placebo null's range (58-82%/41-100%/25-97.5%), not in a tail. **The decay pattern that looked like the strongest finding of the morning is statistically indistinguishable from a generic "inspection paradox" artifact of run-length statistics — it does not appear to reflect anything real about market regimes.** This reverses the morning's ranking: Regime C goes from "most validated" to "least validated" once tested against a proper null. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `regime_c_persistence_debunked_placebo_test`; the morning's `regime_c_persistence_validated_independent_predictor` claim updated in place and marked `STALE` — do not cite it.
3. **NL10/NL30 divergence as a regime-transition leading indicator** (user's own question — "we have that NL30/NL10 stuff from way back, not sure if it's testable/worth it"): it's testable (already-live data, `getNL()` in `server/services/queries.js`, feeds a "momentum weakening" display flag in `confluence.js`/`longterm.js`/`ACDView.jsx` whose predictive validity was never itself backtested) — tested whether divergence/weakening on a currently-trending day predicts a Regime A flip within 5 days. **No signal found**: flip rates are ~identical between diverging/aligned (33.3% vs 33.3%, N=12 too thin either way) and weakening/not-weakening (34.2% vs 32.9%, N=38/76). Forward-return gaps look larger but fail the stability check. Negative finding, recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `nl_divergence_no_regime_transition_signal` — narrow scope, doesn't reopen NL30's already-separately-validated role in session-bias conditioning (Permission Slip system).

## ✅ 2026-07-18 (same day, final): Stage 3 built and run — Regime A fails the independent ground truth, worse than chance on the best-tested configuration. `regime_detection_methodology_needs_validation` RESOLVED.

User said "build it now." Installed `ruptures` into `scratch/.venv` (new dependency). First real snag: a naive BIC-derived penalty (computed from `l2` cost's own scale) did NOT transfer to `rbf` cost at all — identical 83 changepoints across a 4x penalty range, a smoking gun the penalty was nowhere near `rbf`'s real sensitivity range. Fixed with a proper elbow-method sweep (12-20 log-spaced penalty values per model) rather than pushing forward on a miscalibrated number. Per the user's own mid-task redirect ("Use gemini"), dispatched the penalty-selection-and-alignment-test build to Gemini rather than continuing to grind through it directly.

**Audited before trusting, per standing practice — found and fixed one real bug**: the permutation-test script Gemini wrote used `shuffledDates.sort(() => 0.5 - Math.random())`, a known-biased shuffle, not a fair permutation. Re-ran directly with a correct Fisher-Yates shuffle; numbers moved only slightly, conclusion unchanged. Also independently assessed the "rbf elbow = 26 changepoints" claim by reading the full sweep curve directly rather than taking the response's word for it — the curve is genuinely elbow-shaped (flat at 83 for a wide penalty range, then declines to 0), but the specific point chosen sits mid-decline rather than exactly at the flat-to-declining transition — a softer call than the response implied, noted in the spec rather than silently accepted.

**Result**: Regime A's 33 real label transitions were tested against 3 independently-detected changepoint configurations (±3-day tolerance, 500-iteration permutation null). All 3 changepoint-set N's (4/8/17) sit below this codebase's N≥20 floor individually, but the direction is consistent across all three and gets MORE negative as the changepoint set gets more populated: the best-supported configuration (`rbf_26`, N=17) shows Regime A's transitions aligning with real structural breaks *worse* than 78-87% of random date placements would (precision/recall at the 21.6th/13.2th percentile of the null). Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `regime_a_fails_independent_changepoint_alignment`.

**Combined with the same-day findings**: Regime B's trend/stretch buckets are underpowered on non-overlapping data, Regime C's persistence pattern is statistically indistinguishable from a placebo, and now Regime A fails an independent ground-truth test outright — **all three components of Regime A/B/C now have a real, tested negative result, not just an absence of validation.** `OPEN_DECISION` `regime_detection_methodology_needs_validation` **resolved** via `scripts/flag_decision.mjs --resolve` with the full account and a clear recommendation: do not build regime-conditioned sizing on Regime A/B/C as currently constructed. Stage 4 (multiple-comparisons correction) is moot — nothing survived to need correcting. `regimeClassificationService.js`'s header comment and `CLAUDE.md`'s "Where to look" pointers both updated in place to reflect the resolution rather than left pointing at a stale "pending" status. Doesn't rule out regime detection generally (the spec's §3 survey of untried methods — changepoint-based live detection, formal Markov-switching, order-flow signals — remains open for a future attempt); rules out today's specific z-score/tercile construction on this ~410-day history.

Full detail and a 3-step plan to actually validate this (test whether each label predicts something independent of setup EV; sensitivity-test the window/threshold choices; apply a real multiple-comparisons control) is in `OPEN_DECISION` `regime_detection_methodology_needs_validation` (HIGH priority) — **this is the standing next step for this whole thread, do not build anything live off tonight's regime-conditioning findings before it's addressed.**

Softened in urgency, not resolved: the same-night factorial ablation test found the $75 DLL dominates all 3 regime theories combined by more than 2x — so even if the regime metrics turn out to be shaky, the practical stakes of having gotten them wrong are smaller than they first looked.
