     - **`/api/acd/setup-detection` is no longer a concern** — re-measured at a consistent ~1.4s (well within its 14s poll timeout), down from the previously documented ~10-17s. Not a deliberate fix; most open setups already have `touch_quality` cached from earlier in the session, so the new classification block mostly no-ops now. Worth re-checking after a period with more fresh setups.
     - **`/api/performance-audit/unified` (~18s) — root cause is NOT a missing index.** `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the endpoint's main audit query (the `performance_audit` GROUP BY + JOIN) showed it taking **44ms** — not the bottleneck at all, despite the table having grown to 59,772 rows. The real cost is a genuine N+1 round-trip pattern in the "Recent 10-day and 30-day per-level performance (quick replay)" section (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 6888): 4 sequential queries per date × 30 dates ≈ 120 round-trips. Confirmed directly: the single `pdIbQ10` query alone measured 370-565ms per call — ×30 ≈ 11-17s, accounting for nearly all of the observed total.
     - **Attempted fix: batch the 30 dates' queries into 1-2 round trips instead of ~120 — backfired badly, reverted same session.** Rewrote the per-date `dv`/`pdIbQ10`/`pdOrQ10` lookups as a single query using `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` across all 30 unnested dates at once. Verified *logically correct* first (4 sample dates' old-vs-new outputs matched exactly, byte-for-byte) — but `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the real batched query showed **~114 seconds**, ~6x worse than the ~18s original. Root cause: the IB lookup's correlated subquery (`MAX(ts::date) FROM price_bars_primary WHERE ts::date < d.dt ...`), run against `price_bars_primary` — a view over ~40 monthly partitions — defeated partition pruning once wrapped in a `LATERAL` join executed once per outer date row; Postgres fell back to a parallel sequential scan of every partition, 30 times over, plus ~11s of JIT compilation overhead from the resulting enormous plan. **Reverted in the same session** — restored the original per-date sequential-query version, confirmed back to the known ~18s baseline (not worse, not better), no new server errors. Two queries that were still running when the regression was caught (~58s in) were cleared by restarting the server (drops the app's DB connections, Postgres aborts on connection loss) rather than calling `pg_cancel_backend` directly on unverified PIDs, per a permission guardrail — the right call, not a workaround.
     - **Lesson for whoever picks this up next**: batching N per-date queries into one is not automatically a win against this codebase's partitioned `price_bars_primary` view — a correlated/LATERAL construct can defeat partition pruning in a way a simple `WHERE ts::date = ANY($1::date[])` filter (used elsewhere in this same fix, e.g. the bars-batching half, and never actually shown to be the problem) does not. **Always `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` the exact batched query against the real partitioned view before trusting a batching rewrite**, the same way this pass caught the regression before it could sit live — don't assume "fewer round trips = faster" for anything touching `price_bars_primary` without checking the plan first.
     - ~~**Not yet done**: the N+1 pattern in the 30-day replay section~~ — **Fixed 2026-07-15, same session, second attempt.** Root cause of the first attempt's disaster was specifically the correlated IB lookback against the *partitioned* `price_bars_primary` view — the fix avoids that table entirely for this lookup: `IB_HIGH`/`IB_LOW` now come from `level_prices` (precomputed nightly by `scripts/compute_levels.js`, already using the canonical 60-min IB window, on a plain non-partitioned indexed table). `developing_value_log`/`acd_daily_log` lookups (never the slow part) got batched into the same `LATERAL` query since neither is partitioned — no risk there.
       - **Independently verified via Gemini before touching live code** — dispatched a precise, self-contained verification task (not open-ended): run `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the proposed query, and a full 30-date correctness comparison against the original per-date queries. Gemini reported "PROCEED" — `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` under 1ms (independently re-confirmed by Claude directly: 0.966ms, no `price_bars_primary` anywhere in the plan), 24/30 dates matching exactly, 6 dates showing IB high/low discrepancies "as anticipated" in the task prompt.
       - **Audited Gemini's output rather than trusting it outright, per the standing rule — and caught a real error in Gemini's own report.** Gemini's comparison table's values for the 6 flagged dates didn't reproduce when independently re-checked directly (e.g. Gemini reported `2026-06-19 ib_h` old=30644/new=30545.75, but a direct requery showed `2026-06-17`'s values in that slot — an off-by-one date misalignment in Gemini's own comparison script, not a real query bug). Re-ran the comparison directly rather than trust the reported table.
       - **The direct re-check surfaced something more important than value drift: a genuine missing-row gap.** For `2026-06-19`, the OLD (`price_bars_primary`) and NEW (`level_prices`) sources didn't even agree on *which* prior trading day to use as the reference — `price_bars_primary` correctly found `2026-06-18` (61 real bars in the IB window), but `level_prices` had **zero rows at all** for `2026-06-18`, silently falling back to `2026-06-17` instead. Confirmed via `level_prices` row count: 0. This is the exact "`compute_levels.js --backfill` silently skips dates under concurrent DB load" failure mode already documented elsewhere in this codebase's history (docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md), not a new bug — just a new date it happened to. Scanned the surrounding 35-day window for other gaps: only `2026-06-18` (the other flagged "gap," `2026-07-14`, is just today — expected to be missing until tonight's cron, and never gets used as a prior-day reference since it's the most recent date). Fixed directly: `node scripts/compute_levels.js 2026-06-18` — re-verified the `2026-06-19` lookback now matches exactly between both sources.
       - **The remaining 4 dates' small point-level discrepancies (9-50pt) were not investigated further** — both sources agree on which prior day to use for those, just differ slightly in the IB high/low value itself (likely a stale snapshot from before some later data correction, or a minor methodology difference between `compute_levels.js` and an ad-hoc live query — not diagnosed). Given this is an informational 10d/30d replay stat, not a live trading decision, and the definitional/window logic itself is already correct (confirmed via the exact-match dates and the fixed missing-row case), this residual drift was accepted rather than chased further — flagging here rather than silently ignoring it, in case it's worth a fuller `level_prices` freshness audit sometime (a broader version of this exists already in the "checklist-5-9 sweep" large-scope item elsewhere in this file).
       - **Result**: `/api/performance-audit/unified` went from ~18s to **~3.1s** (measured live, post-restart) — the remaining ~3s is the endpoint's many *other* sequential-but-individually-fast queries (this fix only targeted the confirmed dominant N+1 section, never claimed to be the only cost). Verified end-to-end: response shape intact, 49 setups show non-zero `n10d`/`wr10d` (the exact stat this section computes), no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.
   - The 9 `*_backup_20260714` tables (`active_setups_*`, `level_prices_*`) created as safety nets today — see `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `scripts/repair_*.mjs` entry for the full list. Safe to drop once today's fixes have held for a few sessions without needing to roll back.

3. **Large, genuinely new scope (not a continuation of today, needs a dedicated session):**
   - **Touch-quality — price-action pilot RUN 2026-07-15, mixed/inconclusive result, order-flow approach not yet tried.** User asked: can we distinguish a *real touch* (price approaches a level and shows a genuine reaction — absorption, a bounce, a pause) from *price just running through* a level with no real test? Piloted per the plan below on 3 high-N setup_types (`OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` N=295, `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` N=141, `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` N=245) via `scripts/pilot_touch_quality.mjs` — exploratory only, nothing written to `performance_audit`/`active_setups`, no corpus-wide rollout.
       - **`bars_to_first_favorable_tick` does not discriminate at 1-min bar resolution.** 93-99% of reacted trades tick favorable within the very first bar after touch (tercile cuts collapsed to `fast<=1, mid<=1`) — a fade signal, by construction, is entered near a swing extreme, so almost every trade shows *some* favorable wick immediately regardless of whether it goes on to win or lose. This metric isn't the right lens; abandon it rather than force a bucket split that's mostly noise.
       - **The pure "never ticks favorable before stop" bucket is real but small and largely tautological**: n=11/4/1 across the 3 types, 0% WR each — expected, since among `STOP_HIT`/`TARGET_HIT`-only rows, never-favorable within this sample structurally implies `STOP_HIT`. Confirms the concept exists but adds nothing beyond what `resolution` already encodes.
       - **The MAE-magnitude split (adverse excursion accumulated before the first favorable tick — "clean rejection" vs. "slow grind-then-reverse") gave inconsistent, setup-type-dependent results, not a universal filter:**
         - `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`: strong signal in the hypothesized direction — clean-reject WR=76.6% EV=+$17.01 vs. grind-reverse WR=60.8% EV=**-$10.64** (N=124/120). A real, actionable split for this one type.
         - `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`: no meaningful difference — WR=73.3%/71.7%, EV=$2.99/$2.11 (N=146/138).
         - `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG`: **inverted** — grind-reverse actually outperformed (WR=85.9% EV=$13.16 vs. clean-reject WR=72.6% EV=$3.16, N=64/73), the opposite of the hypothesis.
       - **Conclusion: touch-quality via price-action alone is not a clean, corpus-wide filter** — it may be a real per-setup_type refinement (worth keeping `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`-style checks in mind) but doesn't generalize on this 3-type pilot, so no corpus-wide backfill is warranted from this signal alone. Matches the contingency already anticipated below: since it didn't cleanly separate, next step is the order-flow approach, not a bigger price-action rollout.
       - **Order-flow based — RUN 2026-07-15, real corpus-wide signal found, then wired fully live the same day (user: "do what you think is best").** `scripts/pilot_touch_quality_orderflow.mjs` (exploratory pilot, superseded for production use by `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` below). Per explicit user guidance before building this: major structural levels draw genuine two-sided fighting, so heavy volume at a touch does NOT automatically mean "absorbed" — it can mean a fight the adverse side won. The classifier therefore uses 3 buckets, not 2: `HIGH_VOL_ABSORBED` (volume elevated vs. a 90-day trailing per-minute-of-day z-score baseline — reuses the existing `acd.js` VOLUME_SPIKE convention, no new static threshold — AND price holds), `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` (volume elevated but price keeps giving adverse ground anyway), `QUIET` (not elevated). Reaction window is data-derived per setup_type (p25 of that type's own bars-to-resolution).
         - **Piloted on 3 types first** (`OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG`, `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`): `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` was the worst bucket in all 3 (e.g. `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` OVERRUN EV=**-$29.77** vs ABSORBED +$26.42 vs QUIET +$8.92) — consistent enough to justify a corpus-wide check.
         - **Dispatched corpus-wide extension to Gemini** (same exact methodology, N≥50-resolved setup_types, ~40-50 types) — first attempt timed out at the 15-min default (heavy per-setup-type/per-date query load, not a Gemini quality problem — confirmed via `agy` diagnostic: no `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, quick test prompt returned instantly). Retried with the documented 30-min "heavy backtest" budget, completed in 25 min, 47 setup_types.
         - **Independently re-verified by Claude, not just trusted** — re-ran the same script directly (added a day-clustering diagnostic Gemini's pass had skipped despite being asked for it) across all 47 types. Every setup_type Claude could cross-check matched Gemini's numbers **exactly** — real confidence in the methodology replication. **Zero of the 47 types show day-clustering** (top-5-dates share ranges 2-33%, all under the 50% danger line from the CAM_R4/CAM_S3 precedent) — this is not a clustering artifact.
         - **Corrected 2 of Gemini's own classification labels** by re-deriving them from its own reported numbers: `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_LONG` (OVERRUN EV=-$47.40 vs the other two both positive) and `CAM_R3_FADE_LONG` (OVERRUN EV=-$25.21 vs the other two near -$1 to -$2) were both mislabeled `NO_CLEAR_PATTERN` when they clearly fit `OVERRUN_BAD_CONSISTENT` by Gemini's own numbers. Real share of `OVERRUN_BAD_CONSISTENT` is closer to **~45%** of the 47 types, not Gemini's reported 40.4% — a minor correction, doesn't change the conclusion.
         - **Bottom line: `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` (heavy volume + price still gives adverse ground) is a real, common, non-clustered bad signal — but genuinely not universal, confirming the nuance flagged before building this.** ~45% of the 47 setup_types show it as clearly the worst bucket. But a real minority — `OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S1_FADE_SHORT`, `CAM_R2_FADE_SHORT` — show the opposite: `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` performing fine or even best. So this cannot be a blanket corpus-wide rule; any live use needs a per-setup_type check, not a global one.
         - **Wired fully live 2026-07-15, informational only, not silent suppression** — user explicitly confirmed the live schema/route changes via `AskUserQuestion` before any of it touched the live table or live resolution path (this repo's carve-out for hard-to-reverse/shared-state changes, distinct from routine standing-permission actions).
           - **DB**: `active_setups` gained 2 nullable columns, `touch_quality VARCHAR(20)` and `touch_quality_vol_z NUMERIC` (via `server/db.js`, not raw psql — schema.sql regenerated per the documented `pg_dump` command).
           - **Shared logic**: `server/services/touchQuality.js` (`getVolumeBaseline()`, `classifyTouch()`) — used identically by `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` (historical calibration → `performance_audit` `TOUCH_QUALITY` rows, one per N≥50 setup_type: `window_bars`, `high_vol_z_cutoff`, per-bucket N/WR/EV, `thin` flag if bucket N<20) and `acd.js`'s live `resolveSetupsByPrice()` (classifies each open setup once, the moment its calibrated reaction window has elapsed — bars/volume already fetched for the existing resolution walk, no extra query cost — wrapped in try/catch so a classification error can never block real trade resolution). Same margin-based (`>=$3` EV gap, N≥15 non-thin) pattern-tag rule used during the 2026-07-15 audit to catch Gemini's 2 mislabeled types is now encoded in `classifyPattern()` in the calibration script, not just a one-off manual check.
           - **API**: `/api/antigravity/edges-context` (`antigravityEdges.js`) now selects `touch_quality`/`touch_quality_vol_z` on the existing `active_setups` query (additive columns, no `Promise.all` reindexing) and does a **separate** small query for the `TOUCH_QUALITY` calibration rows — deliberately kept outside the documented load-bearing-ordering `Promise.all` array rather than added as a 21st positional entry, since the risk of a silent reindex mistake there outweighs one cheap extra round-trip. Each returned setup gets `touchQualityStats` (bucket + historical WR/EV/N) only when the calibration exists AND that specific bucket isn't `thin` (N≥20) — thin buckets return `null`, so nothing gets shown before there's enough data to trust it.
           - **UI**: `ACDView.jsx`'s live "Today's Actionable Setups" card shows a small badge (⚠/✓/•, green/red by historical EV sign) once `touchQualityStats` is present — purely informational, doesn't affect the existing confidence/recommendation logic.
           - **Cron**: `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` added to `run_weekly_backtests.sh`.
           - **Verified**: server syntax-checked clean, frontend lint/build both pass, server restarted (nodemon hadn't picked up the route/service changes — had to `systemctl --user restart trading-journal-server.service` manually, worth remembering next time a route file changes mid-session), `/api/antigravity/edges-context` returns 200 with the new fields present and correctly `null` pre-calibration, no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` after restart.
           - **Calibration confirmed complete same session**: the initial run was slow under concurrent DB load (live server + dashboard traffic — took ~50 min total vs. a couple minutes when run idle earlier), but finished cleanly — `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM performance_audit WHERE signal_type='TOUCH_QUALITY'` = **47/47**. No further action needed here.
           - **Still not done**: no live setup has actually been observed going through the badge end-to-end yet (needs live market hours + an open setup type that has both a calibration row and a non-thin bucket) — the wiring is verified structurally (endpoint shape, no errors) but not yet observed firing for a real trade. Worth a spot-check next trading session.
           - **Full 8-angle code review run 2026-07-15 immediately after shipping this, on own initiative — found and fixed 10 real issues, several genuine correctness bugs.** Not a routine step; done specifically because this touched live trade-resolution code under real time pressure (a varchar-length bug and a double-backgrounding mistake had already slipped through earlier the same session). All 10 independently re-verified against live code/DB before fixing, not just trusted from the finder pass:
             1. **Bar-boundary mismatch (fixed)** — calibration included the touch bar in its window (`ts >= fired_at`), live excluded it (`ts > fired_at`) — every calibrated cutoff was fit on a systematically different window than it was applied to. Verified empirically (8/8 DB spot-check: `fired_at` always exactly matches a real bar timestamp). Fixed by aligning calibration to live's `>` semantics (not the reverse, to avoid touching the resolution-critical shared bars query).
             2. **Fast-resolving trades never classified (fixed)** — live gate required the *full* calibrated window even after a trade had already resolved with fewer bars; ~25% of trades per setup_type (by construction) got `touch_quality` stuck permanently NULL once they flipped to `RESOLVED` and dropped out of the `active` query. Fixed to classify on whichever bars are available once resolved, matching the calibration script's own windowing exactly (`Math.min(windowBars, availableBars)`).
             3. **`TOUCH_QUALITY` invisible in the Unified Signal Table (fixed, deliberately)** — the 4 new recommendation tags matched none of the table's recognized values, so rows silently vanished (the exact bug class CLAUDE.md's setup-type checklist item 8 exists to prevent). Fixed with an explicit, commented exclusion (not a forced ACTIVE/CONTEXT/REMOVED mapping) — touch-quality is a per-setup_type dimension, not a tradeability verdict; the setup_type's real status already comes from its SETUP_STATUS row.
             4. **RTH-truncated resolution overwrite in calibration (fixed)** — the script's own same-day bar replay was overwriting the real DB `resolution` column, while EV still used the real `actual_pnl`, risking an internally inconsistent WR%/EV$ pair for late-session trades. Fixed to keep the DB's real `resolution` as the win/loss source of truth; the replay now only supplies `barsToResolution`/`maeTrace`.
             5. **Hot-path latency risk (documented, root cause addressed indirectly)** — the touch-quality block runs inside `resolveSetupsByPrice()`, which is polled every **15s with a 14s abort timeout** (worse than this file previously documented as 60s) with no lock against overlapping invocations. Not given a dedicated fix (would need a bigger redesign — e.g. moving classification off the hot path entirely), but the per-day caching consolidation (#9 below) removes the worst-case "lazy 90-day scan mid-loop" cost from every day's first poll to effectively once, which was the dominant risk factor.
             6. **`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` regression from this same session's earlier cleanup (fixed)** — the stale-list fix a few hours earlier had also silently dropped 2 real suppression-mechanism descriptions (TREND counter-direction fade filter, S2 double-counter) that aren't reconstructible from the new live list. Restored both, described qualitatively (no hand-typed WR/N/$, consistent with why they were being cleaned up in the first place) and confirmed both still correspond to real, live code (`isTrendCounterFade`/`isS2DoubleCounter` in `acd.js`). A third dropped entry (`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG`'s specific day-type claim) was **not** restored — couldn't independently verify it's still accurate, and reintroducing an unverified claim would repeat the exact mistake being fixed.
             7. **Calibration script's redundant per-setup_type baseline queries (fixed)** — the 90-day volume baseline and per-date bars only depend on `date`, not `setup_type`, but were being recomputed once per (setup_type, date) pair across the 47 types instead of once per date — the confirmed direct cause of the ~50-minute slow run earlier this session. Fixed with a simple date-keyed cache; re-run after all fixes took **2 minutes**.
             8. **Missing standing rigor diagnostic (fixed)** — the new calibration script never imported the shared `computeRigor()` day-clustering/stability check (`server/services/rigorDiagnostics.js`), violating the explicit "standing, not one-off" rule written after the CAM_R4/CAM_S3 incident. Added per-bucket, informational only (doesn't feed `classifyPattern`), and immediately caught something real on the very first re-run: `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`'s `HIGH_VOL_ABSORBED` bucket is chronologically unstable (EV thirds -21 → +58 → +31), while its `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` bucket is clean/stable. Surfaced in the live badge as a ⚠︎ hover-caution, not a suppression, per the shared module's own convention.
             9. **Duplicate caching mechanism (fixed)** — `getTouchQualityCalib()`/`getTouchQualityBaseline()` had reinvented per-day caching with 4 new module-level variables next to the file's existing generic `getCached`/`setCached` helper. Fixed by giving `getCached` an optional `ttl` parameter and reusing it (`DAY_CACHE_TTL`), removing the duplicate mechanism entirely.
             10. **Baseline cached by wall-clock "today" instead of each setup's own `trade_date` (fixed)** — narrow edge case (a SHADOW/overnight setup classified after midnight ET would fold its own trade date into its baseline instead of excluding it), but real. Fixed by threading `row.trade_date` through to `getTouchQualityBaseline()`, same convention the calibration script already used.
             - **Re-verified end-to-end after all fixes**: lint/build clean, calibration re-run 47/47 in 2 minutes (down from ~50), server restarted, all 3 touched endpoints (`/api/antigravity/edges-context`, `/api/acd/setup-status`, `/api/performance-audit/unified`) return 200, `TOUCH_QUALITY` confirmed absent from the unified table (deliberately), no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.
             - **Historical note**: the `performance_audit` `TOUCH_QUALITY` rows written before this fix pass (i.e. anything queried between initial ship and this re-run) reflected the pre-fix bar-boundary/RTH-truncation bugs — superseded by the re-run, no stale data left in the table (same-day idempotent upsert).
       - **Time-to-resolution — partially answered already, easy to extend.** `backfill_mae_mfe.mjs`'s replay already tracks `bars_to_resolution` per trade. Quick pull 2026-07-14: median time from touch to `TARGET_HIT` across the level-fade family is **7 minutes** (p25=2min, p75=19min, N=3,916). Varies by setup_type: OR/IB fades resolve in 5-11min median, `CAM_R3_FADE_SHORT` runs longer (~15min). Worth extending to a full per-setup_type table, plus the same breakdown for `STOP_HIT` trades (how long does a *losing* trade typically take to reveal itself — symmetric question, not yet pulled).
   - ~~The original checklist-items-5-9 sweep of the long tail of ~100 setup_types beyond the level-fade family~~ — **Partially resolved 2026-07-16**, see the new entry at the top of this file: found and fixed a genuine live regression (IB_BULLISH silently un-suppressed back to ACTIVE); found one bounded-risk orphaned calibration row (MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND); other exemption patterns and standalone pollers checked clean. Not a full manual pass over all 118 types — the automatable slice (exemption-pattern hunting, EV/N consistency, standalone-poller promotion gaps) is done; a full per-type stop/target-simulation review (checklist item 5) for the long tail beyond the level-fade family remains a bigger undertaking if wanted.
   - ~~Execution-quality audit (fill/slippage vs. modeled price, `sizeMultiplier` adherence, stop/target discipline) — 3 of 4 levers from the 2026-07-14 proposal never started.~~ — **Resolved 2026-07-16**, see the new entry at the top of this file: the real blocker turned out to be the trade-to-setup attribution link itself (not the levers) — found and fixed along the way was a `sizeMultiplier` overwrite bug that silently discarded the entire live sizing calculation.
   - ~~IB_BULLISH's session-bias-conflict design decision~~ — **Resolved 2026-07-16**: user decided (extend to level-fades, fold into sizing not suppression) and it's implemented — see the new entry at the top of this file.
   - Delta Divergence Momentum (Gemini's flagship 2026-07-13 finding, N=115, $245/trade EV) — needs its own dedicated `scripts/backtest_delta_divergence_momentum.mjs` before it goes anywhere near live; don't trust Gemini's self-correction alone given the stakes.

4. **Pure time-gated monitors — nothing to do until the date/N threshold hits:** `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` at live N=50, Pulse score at N≥100, `sizeMultiplier` re-audit ~2026-08-20, `AI_SETUP_AGG` ~2026-08-07, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`/`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT` shadow validation ~2026-08-05.

## Morning Prep full performance pass (2026-07-15) — RESOLVED, all endpoints + connection pool fixed

Follow-on from the `/api/performance-audit/unified` fix earlier the same session — user asked to benchmark and fix the remaining ~10 endpoints Morning Prep (`ACDView.jsx`'s dashboard tab) actually calls, plus a flagged 4x-redundant fetch. **Root cause pattern found repeatedly**: `price_bars_primary`'s deduplication view (`GROUP BY`/`array_agg` per minute) forces a full materialization of every matched row before any `MAX()`/`ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` can be taken — cheap when the query has a tight date bound (partition pruning works), catastrophically expensive when it doesn't (Postgres can't prune partitions, has to dedup the *entire* matched history first). This is a different mechanism than the earlier `LATERAL`-defeats-pruning bug, but the same underlying lesson: never query `price_bars_primary` without a date bound if you can help it.

**Fixed, all individually verified byte-for-byte correct against the original query/logic before wiring in, all re-tested live after:**
- **`/api/setups/today`**: 8.1s → 0.095s. Was a correlated `LATERAL` subquery against raw `price_bars` (not even the dedup view) per active_setups row, defeating partition pruning per-row (confirmed via `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`: `loops=35`, scanning every monthly partition per row). Fixed by bulk-fetching the relevant date(s)' bars once and matching in JS — found and preserved a real edge case in the process (a Globex setup's `trade_date` can differ from `fired_at`'s own calendar date; the bar lookup must anchor to `fired_at::date`, not `trade_date`).
- **`/api/auction-read/auto`**: 4.15s → 1.0s. Three separate `price_bars_primary` queries with no lower date bound (`ts::date < $1` / `ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1`, no floor) each forced a ~530K-row dedup materialization. Fixed by adding a generous trailing bound (30 days for "most recent prior date" lookups, 5 days for "single latest bar") — verified identical output, just prunable now.
- **`/api/morning-brief/live-session-context`**: 5.3s → ~3.4s (isolated) / ~8s under real concurrent load (see below). `getTrailing24hrVwapDists()` was a genuine N+1 — one Globex-session-bars query per day (~21-30 sequential round trips). Fixed by bulk-fetching all candidate bars in one query and bucketing into sessions in JS (verified 21/21 distances matched exactly). Also parallelized two independent sequential `computeVAForDate()` calls via `Promise.all`.
- **`/api/morning-brief/forecast`** (→ `getSessionForecast`/`sessionForecastService.js`): 2.84s → ~2.4s (isolated) / ~6.4s under concurrent load. Same unbounded-`price_bars_primary`-lookback pattern (`priorOHLCQ`) fixed with a 30-day floor; also switched that query from raw `price_bars` to `price_bars_primary` (was reading the un-deduplicated table — a consumer missed by the 2026-07-13 dedup-view migration, docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md item 8).
- **`/api/acd/trend-watch`**: checked, already fine (0.456s) — the original 1.77s reading in the first benchmark pass was noise from running immediately after other slow requests in the same sequential test loop, not a real cost. No change made.
- **`/api/antigravity/edges-context` fetched 4x independently on every Morning Prep load** (`PermSlipAndStackBar`, `LivePlaybookCard`, `OvernightContextStrip`, `EdgeSectionsPanel`, each with its own `useEffect`+`setInterval`) — fixed with a new shared-subscription hook, `src/utils/useSharedPollData.js`: multiple components requesting the same URL now share one underlying fetch+poll cycle (fastest-requested interval wins, e.g. `EdgeSectionsPanel`'s 30s). Preserved `EdgeSectionsPanel`'s existing error-surfacing behavior by having the hook return `[data, error]` rather than just `data`. One minor, deliberate behavior change: `LivePlaybookCard`'s websocket-triggered instant refresh (`price-sync-progress`/`setup-detected`) no longer force-refreshes the edges-context portion specifically — it now waits for the shared 30s poll cycle like everyone else, since the fetch is no longer bundled into its own `loadCtx()`. Considered acceptable (30s staleness ceiling, not a correctness issue) rather than building extra machinery to preserve instant-refresh for one field.

~~**Not fixed / found but out of scope tonight:**~~ — **all three picked back up and finished later the same 2026-07-15 session** (user: "i would like too truly finish the speed issues before following code rules cleanup and pushing it up", then after a context-clear interruption: "Whatever is slowing this site and in particular this morning prep page down needs to be sped up"). See the follow-on pass below.
- ~~`getTrailingWeeklyVwapDists()` had the same N+1 shape~~ — **Fixed.** Also found it was being called **twice** from `live-session-context` (once for `weeklyVwapSigma`, once for `weeklyVwapStd`, plus the current week's bars separately re-fetched for `weeklyVwap`/`weeklyVwapSigma`) — all inside `res.json({...})`'s object literal, which JS evaluates property-by-property in declaration order, so 4 `await`-ed fields ran fully sequentially. Fixed both: batched the 12-week query into one bulk fetch (verified byte-identical), and hoisted the current-week-bars + trailing-12-week-dists fetches to run once each via `Promise.all` before building the response object.
- ~~`get14DayRthAtr()`/`getActiveBalanceZone()` already fast~~ — still true individually, but `getSessionForecast()` (`sessionForecastService.js`) had ~10 *other* sequential awaits after them (macro events, prior OHLC, prior value, G-line, monthly pivot, overnight H/L, then a 3-iteration sequential loop for 5/10/20-day range position, then a bracket-age query) — none depend on each other's results. **Fixed**: collapsed all ~12 into one `Promise.all` (the bracket-age query only *looked* like it depended on the range-position loop's result — only its JS post-processing does, the query itself only needs `targetDate`, so it moved into the same batch too).
- ~~`/composite-profile`'s unbounded `latestBar` query~~ — **Fixed**, see the codebase-wide sweep below.

**Full parallelization pass, same session, after the interruption** — applied the same "collapse every independent query into one `Promise.all`" treatment to the two remaining endpoints that were only *partially* fixed above:
- **`/api/morning-brief/live-session-context`**: full handler rewrite — every query beyond the initial bars fetch (`atr20`, `delta30P50`, `trailingRots`, `ibRangePercQ`, `acdRes`, `setupsRes`, `pdRes`, `m1VA`/`m3VA`, `ibRangeCtx`, `pdIbRes`, `pdOrRes`, `ib10Ctx`, `or5Ctx`, `allDayBars`, `trailing24hrDists`, `volBaselineRes`, `trailingDeltas`, plus the weekly-VWAP triplet above) depends only on `date`/`etMin`/`price`/`vwap`, none on each other — collapsed into one ~19-query `Promise.all`. **1.3-1.9s isolated** (down from 3.7s after the first partial fix, ~5.3s originally).
- **`/api/morning-brief/forecast`** (`getSessionForecast`): same treatment, ~12 queries into one batch. **1.6s isolated** (down from 2.7-4.2s).
- **`/api/performance-audit/unified`**: the ~18s→3.1s fix earlier in the session only addressed the 30-date replay's N+1 — the ~18 *other* sequential queries around it (audit/price/ATR/level-price lookups, regime calc, monthly levels, confluence-pairs reads) were still one-at-a-time. Collapsed into one big batch + a second small batch for the 4 queries that genuinely depend on the first batch's results (`regimeQ` needs `currentRegime`, `recentSetups`/`priorAsOfQ`/`allBarsQ` need `recentDates`/`all30Dates`). **1.7-1.8s isolated** (down from 3.1-3.65s). Also found and fixed 3 more instances of the "unbounded `ts::date < $1` lookback defeats partition pruning" pattern while in this code (`pdIbQ` in both the live candidates block and this endpoint's batch, plus `ib10Q`'s inner `DISTINCT` scan) — each ~288ms→126ms, same fix as `/auction-read/auto` got earlier.
- All three re-verified byte-for-byte identical output against pre-fix responses (excluding live-ticking fields like `currentPrice`) before and after each server restart, no new `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` entries.

**The real remaining concurrent-load bottleneck: DB connection pool exhaustion, found and fixed.** The ~7-8s concurrent-load numbers documented above turned out to have a root cause the initial pass didn't catch: **parallelizing each endpoint's internals (the fixes above) made concurrent-load contention worse, not better, until the pool itself was sized for it.** A single real Morning Prep page load fires ~7 endpoints simultaneously; after this session's parallelization work, each of those endpoints now requests ~15-20 DB connections *at once* instead of one-at-a-time — meaning one page load could momentarily demand 100+ simultaneous connections against a pool capped at 20. This showed up as real `"timeout exceeded when trying to connect"` errors in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` during concurrent-load testing (`auction-read`, `scalp-playbook`), not just slowness. The earlier "the DB pool is not the bottleneck" line above was true when each endpoint made one query at a time — it stopped being true the moment this session's own parallelization fixes shipped, which is exactly why it needed re-checking rather than trusted as still valid. Fixed in `server/db.js`: pool `max` 20→60, `connectionTimeoutMillis` 2000→8000 (Postgres `max_connections=100`, ~26 baseline connections in use, so 60 leaves headroom). Concurrent-load wall-clock for the full 6-endpoint batch dropped from ~8.16s to a converging ~3.2-3.5s with repeated runs (OS/Postgres cache warming), zero connection-timeout errors on re-test. Isolated per-endpoint times are now all under 1.9s; concurrent-load times still run ~1.5-3x higher than isolated (expected — Postgres itself, not the app pool, becomes the shared resource once you're past ~60 concurrent connections) but no longer error out.

**Frontend: `PlaybookView.jsx` (1564 lines) converted from an eager top-level import to `lazy()`+`Suspense`**, matching every other view in `App.jsx` — found while investigating the user's "each component just renders so slowly" report. It was the one remaining view still bundled into the main chunk even though it's a separate tab, and `App.jsx` was importing 3 named exports from it (`LevelConfluenceReference`, `ConditionBacktestInline`, `PatternStatsPanel`) that went completely unused in `App.jsx` itself — the already-lazy `ACDView.jsx` imports those same 3 directly from `PlaybookView.jsx` on its own. Main bundle: 1,122KB → 979KB raw (302KB → 273KB gzipped). Verified: build clean, `npm run lint:frontend` clean (one unrelated pre-existing warning in `UpdateDot.jsx`, not from this change), dev server transforms both `App.jsx` and `PlaybookView.jsx` with no errors — real interactive click-through not verified (no Playwright/browser tooling in this environment), so this is a static/transform-level check, not a confirmed working click.

**Also fixed while in this code**: `scripts/hardcoded-threshold-patterns.sh`'s Pattern B (hardcoded setup-type array detector) had a false-positive bug — it matched any line containing the substring `"setup"` anywhere, which caught an unrelated variable name (`setupsRes`) inside a legitimate `[...new Set(...)]` spread and blocked the commit. Narrowed to require the array to open directly on a quoted string literal (`=\s*\[\s*['"]`); verified it still catches the real pattern (`mondaySkip = ['OR_HIGH_FADE', ...]`) and no longer false-positives on spread/computed arrays. This is the single source of truth shared by both the pre-commit hook and the Stop hook, so both are fixed by the one change.

**Verified end-to-end**: lint/build clean throughout, every fix independently confirmed byte-for-byte correct against the original query/logic before deploying, server restarted after each change and re-tested live (isolated + concurrent), pre-commit hook passes clean, no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` across the whole pass (aside from the connection-timeout errors that motivated the pool fix, which don't recur after it).

**Follow-on, same session: "which queries are actually live vs. recomputing something day-stable, and which are slow" — profiled directly rather than guessed.** Of ~16 queries profiled across `live-session-context`/`forecast`/`unified`, only 3 are genuinely live (today's RTH bars, today's Globex bars, current price) — the other 13 all compute something fixed for the entire trading day (trailing ATR/rotation/regime stats, prior-day levels, monthly pivots) and are recomputed from scratch on every poll/page load. **Not yet acted on** — flagged as the next real lever (extend `acd.js`'s existing `getCached`/`setCached` day-cache pattern to `morningBrief.js`/`sessionForecastService.js`) but not implemented this session; would cut real DB round-trips per page load from ~100+ toward near-zero on cache hits, at the cost of a same-day staleness window.

**The slowest single query found in that profiling pass was the "genuinely live, can't cache" one**: `SELECT close FROM price_bars_primary ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1` ("get current price") measured 638-648ms — the same unbounded-lookback bug as everywhere else this session, just never caught because it returns 1 row and looks trivial. Bounding it (`AND ts::date >= CURRENT_DATE - 5`) drops it to 54ms, identical result. **This exact pattern was duplicated in 15 call sites** (`server/routes/weekly.js`, `confluence.js`, `auctionRead.js` — including the `/composite-profile` instance flagged above as "out of scope" earlier the same session, now fixed — `antigravityEdges.js`, `server/services/minuteBarSignalDetector.js`, and 9 separate spots in `acd.js`, including one introduced by this session's own `unified` batching work). All 15 fixed with the same bound. Real measured wins on re-test: `/api/weekly/current` 0.9s→0.1s, `/api/acd/nq/latest` 1.25s→0.08s, `/api/composite-profile` 0.92s→0.28s. Verified byte-for-byte identical output on the 3 endpoints with no branching logic (`composite-profile`, `acd/nq/latest`, `performance-audit/unified`); `/api/weekly/current`'s and `/api/confluence/today`'s outputs differed slightly between before/after snapshots but for unrelated, pre-existing reasons confirmed by direct inspection — `weekly/current` has 3 response-shape branches keyed on whether a `weekly_ib_structure` row already exists for the week, and the "before" call itself inserted the row that made the "after" call take a different (data-equivalent) branch; `confluence/today`'s only diff was its own `calculatedAt` timestamp. Not bugs from this fix.

## Pending decisions / unconfirmed proposals

- **⚠️ CORRECTION NOTICE (2026-07-14): every dollar-EV figure dated 2026-07-14 elsewhere in this file, for any `resolution_method='BACKFILL'` setup_type, was computed at the wrong $/pt scale — read this before trusting any dollar figure below.** `scripts/archive/backfill_level_fades.js` (and every `scripts/repair_*.mjs` script that copied its convention today) used `PT=5, COMM=5` — but the live resolution path (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 155, `const PNL_PER_POINT = 2; // MNQ = $2/point`) is explicit that the real contract is MNQ at $2/point, $1 commission. $5/pt matches neither MNQ ($2) nor standard NQ ($20) — an uncaught error in the archived script from the start, not a deliberate convention (user confirmed: "not sure why you picked $5"). **The error only affects the magnitude of dollar-EV figures for `BACKFILL`-sourced setup_types (overstated ~2.5x) — it does NOT change which setups are winners vs. losers (sign held in every case checked), and does NOT affect the 32+14=46 `SETUP_STATUS` recommendation flips' underlying *direction* claims, though some of those flips' exact classification (`SUPPRESS` vs `ACTIVE`/`PROMOTE`) did shift once rescaled, since the standard `-$5` `SUPPRESS_MAX_EV` threshold is itself calibrated at the correct $2/pt scale (it was already being applied correctly to the non-BACKFILL "PRICE_CLEAN"/live-resolved family all along).** Fixed via `scripts/repair_dollars_per_point.mjs` (backup: `active_setups_pnl_rescale_backup_20260714`) — rescaled `actual_pnl` directly from each row's already-correct point distances (`stop_level`/`t1_level`/`entry_zone_low`), no need to re-run any of today's detection/resolution logic. All 6 `repair_*.mjs` scripts also corrected at the source (`PT=2, COMM=1`) so re-running them in the future won't reintroduce the bug. Recalibrated `backtest_setup_status.mjs` + `update_optimal_stops.mjs` afterward — **14 more recommendation flips**, several `SUPPRESS→ACTIVE`/`PROMOTE→SUPPRESS` (the rescaling moved several near-boundary EVs across the `-$5` threshold in both directions): `CAM_S1_FADE_SHORT`, `FLOOR_R1_FADE_LONG`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`, `OR_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG`, `PD_VAH_FADE_LONG`, `PD_POC_FADE_LONG`→`ACTIVE`; `CAM_R1_FADE_SHORT`, `CAM_S1_FADE_LONG`, `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`→`SUPPRESS`; `IB_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `PD_VAL_FADE_LONG`→`PROMOTE` (⚠ same trailing-90-day caution as before — not cleared for live); `PD_POC_FADE_SHORT`→`PROMOTE`; `OPEN_DRIVE_LONG` settled back to `SUPPRESS`. **Corrected final EV for the two original investigation targets**: `CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT` N=114 EV=**-$12.95** (still `SUPPRESS`, was wrongly shown as -$34.87), `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` N=141 EV=**+$7.98** (still `ACTIVE`, was wrongly shown as +$17.45) — same sign, correct real magnitude. **Not done**: did not go back and edit every individual dollar figure already written into the CAM_R4/CAM_S3 entry and others below — too much prose to safely edit without introducing transcription errors; treat every dollar-EV number dated 2026-07-14 elsewhere in this file as directionally correct but ~2.5x overstated in magnitude for `BACKFILL`-sourced types, and use this corrected notice + a fresh `performance_audit` query as the source of truth going forward, not the historical prose.

- ~~**Execution-quality audit (proposed 2026-07-14) — 3 of 4 levers never started.**~~ — **Resolved 2026-07-16**, see the new entry at the top of this file for the full account. Short version: lever 1 (detection latency) unchanged from below. Levers 2 (fill/slippage) and 4 (stop/target discipline) turned out to be blocked on a real, deeper problem — the only trade-to-setup attribution link (a 5-min time-proximity match) recovers only ~4 real matches across the *entire* trade history once direction and price-proximity are correctly checked, far below N≥20. Lever 3 (sizing-multiplier adherence) uncovered a genuine live bug instead: the elaborate ~20-factor level-fade `sizeMultiplier` was being silently overwritten by a crude post-loss binary flag before ever reaching the DB or the live API response — fixed.

- **IB_BULLISH live incident (2026-07-14) — root-caused and fixed same day, two follow-ups still open.** Live `IB_BULLISH` (fired 09:58 ET, entry 29783/stop 29704/T1 29879) hit its stop for -$159 while every other context signal on the dashboard (overnight `SHORT TRAP`, Permission Slip 69% SHORT N=171, 3 session-signal stats all 72-74% SHORT, Claude's own "Ask Claude" read) was calling for SHORT/WAIT. Investigation found: (1) `IB_BULLISH`'s blended live EV was -$27.81/trade (N=106) — a real structural loser, not bad luck; (2) `backtest_setup_status.mjs` was unconditionally skipping the standard SUPPRESS check for `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` (the `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` set) and deferring to per-day-type gating in `acd.js` instead — but that gating (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 3762, `if (dtClass === 'BALANCE' && ibSetup) ibSetup = null;` etc.) depends on `acd_daily_log.day_type`, which isn't classified until IB close at 10:30 ET, while `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` fire off a deliberate 30-min IB window and could fire as early as 10:00 — so the day-type check was a guaranteed no-op every time it mattered; (3) a set of in-code comments (2026-07-07 Opus Audit 2 vintage) claiming IB_BULLISH's TREND-day EV was "+$16 EV solid" had silently drifted to -$16 EV by 2026-07-14 — same silent-drift pattern as prior incidents, just in a comment instead of a live string this time. **Fixed same session:** moved the IB_BULLISH/IB_BEARISH fire gate from `etMin>=600` to `etMin>=630` (acd.js) so `dtClass` is always known by fire time (30-min IB *level* definition unchanged, still spec); extended `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` handling to compute real per-day-type EV and fall through to `SUPPRESS` if every bucket with N≥20 is below the standard `-$5` bar (reuses existing constants, no new thresholds) — `IB_BULLISH` now correctly shows `SUPPRESS` (all buckets confirmed negative: BALANCE N=53 EV=-$47, TREND N=34 EV=-$16), `IB_BEARISH` correctly stays `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED` (TURBULENT N=30 EV=+$78 is genuinely strong); wired `_suppressedSetups` into the `ibSetup` candidates-array check (`acd.js` ~line 5293) alongside the existing DOW check; corrected all 3 stale in-code comment blocks with today's verified numbers.

User chose: extend to the level-fade family, fold into `sizeMultiplier` as a real size-down factor (not suppression), backtest the delta first rather than guess. See the new entry at the top of this file for the backtest (N=4,037 CONFLICT vs N=1,887 NO_CONFLICT, $43.55/tr delta, confound-checked) and the live wiring.
  - **Still open (2): broader audit of all setup types requested by the user 2026-07-14** ("we might need to look at the guts of all my setups to be sure they are working as expected") — **partially addressed 2026-07-16**, see the new entry at the top of this file. Found and fixed a real regression (IB_BULLISH's `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` recovery logic had silently un-suppressed it back to `ACTIVE` despite EV=-$29/trade) and one bounded-risk stale calibration row (`MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND`). Exemption-pattern hunting, standalone-poller promotion gaps, and Unified Signal Table status-mapping were checked systematically (all clean beyond the one regression); a full manual per-type stop/target-simulation pass (checklist item 5) across the long tail beyond the level-fade family is still not done — flagged as the honest remaining scope if a deeper pass is wanted.
    - **Methodology that made today's pass work — repeat this, don't shortcut it:** (a) query live numbers directly (`active_setups`, `performance_audit`) rather than trusting an existing `recommendation` label, a code comment, or a prior session's stated conclusion — the IB_BULLISH comments claiming "+$16 EV solid" were from a real 2026-07-07 audit that had simply gone stale, and would have been trusted at face value without a direct requery; (b) specifically hunt for *exemption* patterns — anywhere a setup type is carved out of the standard pipeline check (like `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` skipping `SUPPRESS`) — and verify the carve-out's own replacement mechanism can actually fire before the setup's own decision point, not just that a check exists in the code; (c) when fixing, extend/reuse the existing pipeline and its existing constants (`SUPPRESS_MIN_N`, `SUPPRESS_MAX_EV`) rather than adding a new hardcoded check to `acd.js`; (d) when building something new that could affect live firing (like the session-bias cross-check below), default to a visible flag, not silent suppression, until it's been observed for a while; (e) after every change, verify with a direct query AND a live endpoint curl — don't stop at "syntax check passed" or "no crash on save" (a transient nodemon-restart-window error surfaced once during today's work and was confirmed harmless only by retrying live, not by assumption); (f) correct stale comments/claims when found, not just the specific number that triggered the investigation — three separate in-code comment blocks had drifted, not one.
    - Candidate approach for the mining side specifically: Gemini for the data-mining half (per-setup live EV pulls, day-type/DOW breakdowns) per the Gemini-for-mining/Claude-for-implementation convention, Claude for the code-path verification half (does each setup's suppression actually get a chance to run before it fires, per (b) above) — mirrors how this specific incident was solved. Audit Gemini's pulled numbers the same way (b) says to audit existing code comments — don't skip that step just because a different agent produced the number this time.
    - **Priority order decided 2026-07-14, superseded same day (see the "CAM_R4/CAM_S3 investigation" thread below for what actually happened):** the original plan was to check `CAM_R4_FADE_SHORT`/`CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` first, then the rest of the then-current top-8 by EV. That investigation instead surfaced 2 systemic data bugs (duplicate-bar contamination across all 72 BACKFILL-sourced setup_types, and a backtest-window-vs-live-firing-window mismatch verified so far only for CAM_R4/CAM_S3) and both are now partially fixed — see that thread for the full account, including the 13 real ACTIVE→SUPPRESS flips this produced. **Updated priority order for the next session**: (1) re-verify the window mismatch (bug 2) for the rest of the *current* top-8-by-EV (re-pull the ranking fresh — it moved after today's recalibration), being careful with formation-time-dependent levels (IB/OR) to avoid introducing lookahead; (2) only after that, sweep the remaining setup_types more lightly per the original checklist-items-5-9 plan.

- ~~CAM_R4/CAM_S3 investigation (opened 2026-07-13)~~ — **Resolved 2026-07-14 into a full-corpus repair.** The original 111-vs-56 count-gap question turned out to be moot (a lost ad-hoc scratch script from a prior session, not a real bug) — but investigating it properly surfaced four real, verified data-integrity bugs affecting the entire level-fade backfill corpus (72 setup_types, every `resolution_method='BACKFILL'` row in `active_setups`, 6,375 rows final). All four are now fixed for the full corpus (all 34 base level families, zero exclusions).
  1. **Duplicate-bar contamination — fixed for all 72 setup_types.** `scripts/archive/backfill_level_fades.js` queried raw `price_bars` directly instead of the deduped `price_bars_primary` view (the duplicate-minute-bar fix from 2026-07-13, docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md item 8) — it predates that fix and was never re-run. Fixed via `scripts/repair_backfill_duplicate_bars.mjs` (backup: `active_setups_backfill_backup_20260714`).
  2. **Backtest-window (10:30am-noon) vs. live-firing-window (any RTH touch, ~9:34 AM on) mismatch — fixed for all 34 base level families, in 5 waves.** Live `acd.js` (`nearLevels`, ~line 4757) has no window restriction; the archived backfill script does. Re-simulating "first touch anywhere in RTH" against clean data, using the *correct formation gate per level type* (not a blind 9:30 start — that would introduce lookahead for same-day-forming levels):
     - **Wave 1** (`scripts/repair_cam_r4_s3_window_mismatch.mjs`): `CAM_R4`, `CAM_S3` (gate 570, prior-session Camarilla pivots).
     - **Wave 2** (`scripts/repair_top8_window_mismatch.mjs`): `PD_OR_MID`, `PD_IB_HIGH`, `PD_POC`, `FLOOR_S1`, `CAM_R1`, `CAM_S2`, `CAM_S4` (gate 570), `OR_LOW` (gate 575 — OR forms from bars 570-574, valid at 9:35 ET, `acdService.js` ~line 359).
     - **Wave 3** (`scripts/repair_remaining_window_mismatch.mjs`): `OR_HIGH` (gate 575), plus 20 more prior-period-derived families at gate 570: `PD_VAH`, `PD_VAL`, `FLOOR_PIVOT`, `FLOOR_R1`, `PD_IB_MID`, `PD_IB_LOW`, `PD_SESSION_MID`, `5D_OR_MID` (confirmed via its own query using `trade_date < $1` — strictly backward-looking despite being labeled `CURRENT` category in `compute_levels.js`; verified the actual WHERE clause, not the label), `WS1`, `WS2`, `WR1`, `WR2`, `WPP`, `MPP`, `MR1`, `MR2`, `MS1`, `MS2`, `CAM_R2`, `CAM_R3`, `CAM_S1`.
     - **Wave 4** (`scripts/repair_ib_dependent_window_mismatch.mjs`): `IB_HIGH`, `IB_LOW`, `IB_MID` (`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE`), `OR_MID` (`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE`) at gate 630 (10:30 ET), plus a redo of `PD_IB_HIGH`/`PD_IB_LOW` (waves 2/3 had fixed their window-timing but with stale level values — see bug 3 below) — run only after bug 3 was fixed. Re-run a second time after discovering bug 4 below (some `PD_IB_HIGH`/`PD_IB_LOW` `level_prices` rows were still stale the first time).
     - **Wave 5** (`scripts/repair_weekly_vwap_window_mismatch.mjs`): `WEEKLY_VWAP` (gate 570) — only after fixing its lookahead bug (bug 4 below).
     - Formation-gate classification cross-verified two independent ways before waves 3-5 ran: Claude read `scripts/compute_levels.js` directly; Gemini was independently dispatched the same classification task (`scratch/antigravity_response.md`) — both agreed on every row, including two non-obvious findings Claude found first and Gemini corroborated: IB (as `compute_levels.js` had it) forms at et_min 600 (10:00 ET), not the 630 (10:30 ET) assumed from the day-type-classifier convention elsewhere in this codebase — which turned out to matter, see bug 3; and `WEEKLY_VWAP` has a genuine lookahead bug in its own formula, see bug 4.
  3. **`IB_HIGH`/`IB_LOW` definitional mismatch, found and fixed while chasing bug 2's IB exclusion.** Live `acd.js` computes today's IB from a **60-minute** window (bars 570-629, gated `etMinNow>=630`, `server/routes/acd.js` ~line 4404-4419); `scripts/compute_levels.js` (source of `level_prices`, what historical backfills read) computed IB from a **30-minute** window (bars 570-599) — genuinely different price levels, not just different availability times. Git history: the 60-minute definition in `acd.js` predates `compute_levels.js` by a full month (2026-06-01 vs 2026-07-01) and matches this codebase's documented day-type-classifier convention ("Initial Balance closes" at 10:30 ET, `docs/daytype_classifier_v2_candidate.md`) — strong circumstantial evidence the 30-minute version was the bug, though not a certainty against original design intent (flagged to the user as an unconfirmed judgment call before acting — **user confirmed standardizing on 60-minute**). Fixed at the source: `scripts/compute_levels.js`'s `IB_HIGH`/`IB_LOW`/`IB_MID` and `PD_IB_HIGH`/`PD_IB_LOW`/`PD_IB_MID` blocks now both use bars 570-629; full historical `level_prices` re-backfilled via `node scripts/compute_levels.js --backfill` (backed up first to `level_prices_ib_backup_20260714`).
  4. **`WEEKLY_VWAP`'s own formula had a genuine lookahead bug — found and fixed.** `compute_levels.js` ~line 304-311 computed it via `ts::date BETWEEN wb.mon AND wb.fri` — i.e., through that **week's Friday close**, not just through the date being computed — so any `level_prices.WEEKLY_VWAP` row for a date before that week's Friday reflected volume-weighted price data that hadn't happened yet relative to that date. Fixed by changing the end bound to `date` (now a legitimate week-to-date VWAP as of each date); full historical `level_prices` re-backfilled (backup: `level_prices_weeklyvwap_backup_20260714`).
  5. **Both `--backfill` runs (bugs 3 and 4) silently skipped ~40-41 dates each under concurrent DB load, with no visible error in the piped/tailed console output — found and fixed.** Discovered by spot-checking a date against a raw SQL recomputation and finding it still matched the *pre-fix* formula despite the backfill reporting success. The per-date `try/catch` in `compute_levels.js --backfill`'s loop swallows failures to `console.error`, invisible when output is piped through `tail`. Diagnosed via `level_prices.computed_at` staleness (any row not touched on run day was suspect) and fixed by re-running `compute_levels.js <date>` individually for each stale date (all succeeded standalone, confirming the failures were concurrency-related — likely contention with the live server's 60s poller and the many other scripts run against the same DB this session — not a real per-date computation bug). **Lesson, now in CLAUDE.md**: never trust a truncated/piped console summary as proof a `--backfill` run is complete; verify with a freshness check across the full expected date range.
  6. **Found and self-healed, root cause not fully identified: all 98 `WEEKLY_VWAP_FADE_LONG`/`SHORT` rows vanished from `active_setups` sometime between the duplicate-bar fix (bug 1) and the window-mismatch fix (bug 2/wave 5).** Confirmed isolated to just this one setup_type (4 other recently-suppressed setup_types spot-checked, all had expected row counts intact) and confirmed NOT caused by `expireStaleSetups()`'s SHADOW-row cleanup (only deletes `status='SHADOW'` rows; every backfill-sourced row is inserted already-`RESOLVED`/`EXPIRED`). Self-healed by wave 5's own full backup+delete+reinsert cycle, so no data was permanently lost, but worth a quick row-count sanity check on other setup_types in a future session if anything looks unexpectedly thin.

SUPPRESS/THIN_N→ACTIVE (real recoveries): `5D_OR_MID_FADE_SHORT`, `CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` (N nearly doubled to 248 once the correct 60min IB was used, EV flipped -$28.94→+$5.29), `MPP_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_OR_MID_FADE_SHORT`, `WPP_FADE_SHORT`. `CAM_R1_FADE_SHORT`, `CAM_S1_FADE_LONG`, `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`, `PD_POC_FADE_LONG`→`PROMOTE` (⚠ trailing-90-day recovery, all-time EV still negative for all 4 — same caution as `OPEN_DRIVE_LONG`'s earlier razor-thin promotion, this bucket also oscillated PROMOTE↔SUPPRESS across recalibration waves as sample composition shifted slightly each time; worth a manual look, don't treat as cleared-for-live). `OR_HIGH_FADE_LONG` and `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` both went ACTIVE→`THIN_N` (N dropped to 18 and 10 respectively once duplicate/mistimed/wrongly-defined fires were removed — legitimate sample-size corrections, not red flags; `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG`'s original N=81 was mostly a window-definition artifact). `OPEN_DRIVE_LONG` settled at `SUPPRESS` (its earlier razor-thin `PROMOTE` didn't survive full recalibration).

  - ~~**NOT done / follow-up needed: query performance on `active_setups`-heavy endpoints has degraded**~~ — **Re-checked 2026-07-16, not currently reproducible.** Direct timing: `/api/performance-audit/unified` ~1.2-1.4s, `/api/acd/setup-detection` <2ms (the latter is served from its existing 20s response cache — real cold cost not separately isolated, but neither endpoint is anywhere near the 41s/17s previously logged). Likely explained by other fixes landing later in the 2026-07-15/16 sessions (DB pool resize, the `Promise.all` batching pass) after the 41s/17s reading was taken. Dispatched to Gemini for root-cause diagnosis first; both of its specific theories (an N+1 loop calling `getSetupStats` per setup, and a missing `setup_type` index causing a slow momentum-lookback query) were checked directly via `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` and didn't hold up — `getSetupStats` has exactly one call site (on-demand, not a loop) and the momentum-lookback query already runs in 0.19ms thanks to `idx_as_unique_setup`'s `trade_date` ordering satisfying the `LIMIT 10` before a full scan is needed. `active_setups` genuinely has no index leading with `setup_type` alone, but nothing currently measured needs one. Worth a fresh re-measurement if the "trending the wrong way" pattern reappears as the BACKFILL corpus keeps growing, rather than assuming this note is still accurate.
